# AI Market BBB — 1 November 2026 (re-marked 7 November 2026; K1 capex cycle re-run 11 November 2026)

**Status, 11 Nov 2026 (re-run under skill `ai-market-bbb` 2026-11-11.2; user: "Yes, apply D"):**
the K1 Base is now the most likely path, set by the new **K1 capex cycle** section: contracted 2027
(1,120), then a digestion plateau (1,170 in 2028 and 2029). H1 compute (730 / 750 / 745), H4
(125 / 129 / 130), their Base EPS paths, the E cash conversion (−.20 / .11 / .36) and the
monetisation ratio (0.24 / 0.31) follow from it. H2, H3, E1, E2, T1–T4, Bear and Bull are
unchanged. Prices are Update 7's (6 Nov close), not re-marked, so the basket changes are the method
effect alone: all six **9.2%** Base NOK, compute **7.5%**, platforms **8.6%**, networking **−2.7%**.
The dated notes below are earlier states; the history of this re-run is in the Change log.

## 5 Nov 2026, kept unchanged: the US midterms and the 4–5 Nov chip sell-off

**AI Market BBB kept unchanged.** No Base, Bear or Bull path, revenue index, exit P/E or cash
conversion moves. Skills: `ai-market-bbb` 2026-11-01.1, `iaf-valuation` 2026-11-01.1. Upstream,
read as they stand: Equity BBB 17/18 Oct (it has not read the midterms yet; NOK path kept) and HY
BBB 5 Nov (NIBOR path unchanged; HY market Base 6.8% → 7.2%, reference E / R 0.196 → 0.254). The
detail is under Drivers ("US midterms: the policy channels") and in the Memory cycle, section 5.
Prices: US intraday 5 Nov 19:36 UTC (session open), Seoul and Xetra closes 5 Nov.

- **Trigger:** Democrats won the House and the Senate on 3 Nov, and Democrats who pledged
  data-center pauses won the governorships of Texas, Georgia and Ohio. On 4 Nov the platforms rose
  (Microsoft +6.7%, Amazon +6.1%) while Texas power fell (Vistra −13.8%, NRG −13.5%) and the SOX
  fell 3.1%. On 5 Nov the SOX fell ~10%, the Nasdaq-100 ~5.6%, Micron ~15%, AMD ~15%, NVIDIA ~8.5%
  and the TSMC ADR ~9%. Seoul fell first (SK hynix −9.5%, Samsung −7.6%, Kospi −5.3%); IQQK −9.0%
  on Xetra. No company news was found; Qualcomm's weak outlook was a side factor `[E]`.
- **Verdict: a price event (a de-rating of the capex receivers), not a change to the Base
  fundamentals.**
  1. **Federal:** nothing changes in law before the new Congress sits (3 Jan 2027). After that,
     bills need the President's signature (no veto-proof majority) and 60 Senate votes. A national
     moratorium (Sanders–Ocasio-Cortez) cannot pass, and tax expensing stays. The likeliest
     bipartisan bill, grid-cost allocation, raises the cost per MW, which the hyperscalers already
     pledged to bear (White House, Mar 2026); it does not cut volume. Export bills hit China sales
     that NVIDIA's guidance already excludes.
  2. **States are the real channel, and they act slowly.** The governors take office on 11–19 Jan
     2027, and statutory bans need the legislatures. Texas (~27% of planned US data-center GW) has
     had Abbott's own pause on new loads ≥ 75 MW since 3 Aug, and the hyperscalers guided 2027
     capex up in late October with that pause in force.
  3. **No hyperscaler, NVIDIA or utility changed guidance after the result, and no broker capex cut
     was found.**

  The 2027 build is contracted and under construction, so K1 2027 (1,120) holds. The risk is 2028
  (Base +12%): new US sites slip or move to other states and abroad, at a higher cost. An
  illustrative slip (half of the new 2028 sites in Texas, Georgia and Ohio six months late, half of
  them re-sited) takes ~3–4% off K1 2028 `[A]`, below the 5% rule and inside the Base range.
- **Risk added, not points:** state siting policy is a second route to the Bear's 2028 capex cut,
  beside the financing break. It is now a signpost (Signposts and monitoring).
- **Signposts:** none crossed. CoreWeave made a new 2026 low ($43.90 intraday, −68% from its May
  high): the first leg of the neocloud Bear signpost. The credit leg is not measured, so it is read
  as touched. New dated
  signposts:
  - NVIDIA Q3 FY27 (17 Nov);
  - the ERCOT/PUCT data-center audit (10 Dec);
  - the FY27 stopgap (11 Dec, current Congress; context only);
  - the new Congress (3 Jan 2027);
  - the Ohio and Georgia governors (11 Jan), the Texas Legislature (12 Jan) and the Texas governor
    (19 Jan);
  - the hyperscalers' first 2027 capex figures (late Jan–early Feb).
- **Memory cycle: the view is unchanged** (late upcycle; Base turn in 2028). There is no new
  contract-price, LTA or capex news. **At the 5 Nov prices the basket earns k at 0.80× Base
  2028–30 (0.97× on 30 Oct) and 0% at 0.52× (0.65×). The price now discounts a deeper or earlier
  turn than Base, about 20% below it.** Per name: SK hynix 0.66× (0.77×), Samsung 0.75× (0.86×),
  Micron 1.05× (1.43×).
- **Baskets at the 5 Nov prices** (Base NOK, method unchanged; 30 Oct in brackets): all six 10.1%
  (8.0%); compute 11.8% (7.4%) and foundry 11.7% (6.7%), both just under their hurdle P/E (margin
  of safety −0.7% and −0.8%); memory 19.4%
  (12.9%); platforms 8.0% (8.8%); networking 1.4% (−2.3%); software 3.4% (0.8%). The Nordic HY
  market is now 7.2% (reference E / R 0.254).
- **Downstream:** no path that any document reads moved. On prices only, the memory basket row
  (kospi) and the price-implied multipliers (skh, smsn, kospi, mu) moved.

## Conclusion

- **K1 capex cycle (11 Nov re-run, skill 2026-11-11.2): K1 Base 2028–29 is a digestion plateau.**
  - **Why:** Base is the most likely path, not the companies' plan. 2027 is contracted (1,120).
    After it, both earlier hyperscaler surges ended in a flat year (2019 +4%, 2023 −2%) `[F]`; E2
    slows by 25–28 pp from 2026 to 2028 on its own Base; NVIDIA's supply commitments are flat in
    FY28–29 `[F]`. On the Base E-paths the AI asset base earns ~2.5% pre-tax in 2028 and ~0% in
    2029: the revenue needed for 10% is covered 70% and 63% (return gauge). So Base holds K1 at
    **1,170 in 2028 and 2029** (+4.5%, 0%). The financing break stays the Bear (950 / 800 / 820).
  - **Carried through:** H1 compute 730 / **750 / 745** (H1/K1 ratio held) with Base EPS
    .92 / **.91 / .88 / .87**; H4 125 / **129 / 130** with EPS .95 / **.94 / .94 / .99**; E cash
    conversion −.20 / **.11 / .36** (the five's capex is K1); monetisation ratio **0.24 / 0.31**.
    H2 is kept: its 2028 turn already assumes this digestion. H3's knock-on is below the minimum
    change. E1, E2 and T1–T4 are unchanged: the plateau is digestion of capacity, not a demand
    shortfall.
  - **Baskets (Base NOK, 6 Nov prices, so the method effect alone):** all six **9.2%** (Bear −6.7%,
    Bull 20.7%); compute **7.5%** (hurdle P/E 12.1 against 13.7, margin of safety −11.8%, E / R
    0.068); platforms **8.6%** (E / R 0.110); networking **−2.7%**. Memory (21.6%), foundry (10.2%)
    and software (3.7%) are unchanged. The compute basket earns k only at 1.14× its Base 2028–30
    path, the platforms at 1.10×.
- **11 Nov note: AI Market BBB kept unchanged.** CoreWeave's Q3 leans Base on the neocloud
  signpost (neither line). TSMC's October (+59.7% y/y) is on the Bull side, not crossed. Anthropic's
  Q3 ($17.0bn booked) fits the E1 2026 build. Its range ($70–78) would cross the lab-financing Bull
  line if it prices inside it (~18 Nov). No path, basket value or price moved.
- **Update 7 (7 Nov, small): AI Market BBB paths kept unchanged.** The Equity BBB of 6 Nov changes
  only World's comparison values (Base 6.6%, E / R 0.080) and the NOK spot (relative moves
  unchanged), and the HY reference is 0.254. AMD's and Arista's Q3 reports and TrendForce's 4 Nov
  spot prices were re-tested, and none moves a point. At the 6 Nov close all six baskets return
  10.0% (Base NOK). Memory returns 21.6%, and its price implies 0.75× Base 2028–30. Details are in
  the note at the top. The Update 6 text follows.
- **Update 6 (1 Nov): AI Market BBB paths kept unchanged. A memory-cycle assessment is added, and
  Amazon's Q3 is taken in. Returns rose because prices fell on 30 Oct.**
  - **The memory cycle** (new section "H2 Memory cycle", below the hardware interface; it is the
    lead section for the Samsung, SK hynix and IQQK analyses):
    - **Where:** late upcycle. Contract prices still rise (Q4-26 conventional DRAM +10–15%, ~+20%
      with HBM; Q1-27 +3–8%), but q/q growth has halved twice since Q2-26. Mobile DRAM (0–5%) and
      NAND outside enterprise SSD are about flat. Supplier inventory is under two weeks, and margins
      are records (SK hynix 80% operating). The shares peaked in June: SK hynix −51%, Samsung −38%,
      Micron −27%, Kospi −37%.
    - **What turns it:** new bits from 2H-27 into 2028 (new fabs, the 1δ / G11 nodes, CXMT +31% in
      wafers, HBM wafers returning to DRAM if HBM growth slows). They meet AI capex digestion after
      the contracted 2027 build and a consumer market that is already shrinking at today's prices.
      The LTAs soften the fall for the leaders, but they cap the rise, and SK hynix and Samsung
      disclose no floors.
    - **Against Base (turn in 2028): Base kept.** Timing is about Base, with the risk 1–2 quarters
      earlier for NAND and commodity DRAM (2H-27). The 2028 depth (.60) is Base or shallower for
      the leaders. 2029–30 (.40 / .35) is Base or deeper: the 2028–29 supply wave is larger than
      2017–19's.
    - **What the price implies:** at the 30 Oct closes the memory basket (3.3× CY27) earns k if
      2028–30 EPS is **0.97× Base**, and 0% at **0.65× Base**. **The price discounts the Base turn,
      not an earlier or deeper one.** SK hynix prices a path 23% below Base, Samsung 14% below, and
      Micron 43% above. Depth decides, not timing: a turn half a year earlier costs ~2 pp, a year
      later adds ~6 pp.
  - **Falsification attempt (Base), hint by hint:**
    1. **E-layer company check: .92 kept.** Amazon v2 is .910 of the 1 Nov consensus ($10.65;
       .919 of $10.55). With the post-report consensus (Microsoft 22.08 → .941; Alphabet 14.84 →
       .854) and the rest row (.97), the 30 Oct blend is **.914 / 1.033 / 1.150 / 1.258**. The gaps
       are −0.006 / −0.037 / −0.020 / −0.012, so all Base points are kept. This confirms DNB
       Teknologi's ≈ .91.
       - Bear blend .774 / .729 / .804 / .887: the 2027 gap is **−0.046**, just inside the 0.05
         rule; it is the next point to watch.
       - Bull gaps are ≤ −0.025, and cash conversion is ≈ −.23 / −.04 / .16 against −.20 / .00 /
         .20: all kept.
       - Sensitivity: with Meta on the analysed platforms' Bear, the Bear 2027 gap would be
         −0.063. The rule keeps the old path for the unanalysed rest, so a Meta analysis is the
         fix.
    2. **E2: kept.**
       - AWS Q3 $46.0bn (+39.3%); CY26 ~$175.8bn (+36.6%).
       - The four clouds' 2026 is ~440bn, **~+53%** `[A]`, against the row's ~+50%: 3 pp in the
         current year, kept.
       - 2027 bottom-up ~+41% (~+42% on Amazon's "over 40%") against +38%: below the 5 pp rule.
       - 2028: Amazon's CEO projects AWS "over 40%" against the row's +25% and the Bull's +32%. That
         is company guidance `[E]`, and the Amazon analysis itself keeps AWS at the row, so it is a
         Bull-side signpost, not a move.
    3. **K1: kept (790 / 1,120).** Amazon kept ~220bn for 2026; 2027 is to "meaningfully
       increase", with no figure.
    4. **K4:** the AWS backlog is $578bn (+16.5% q/q), so the total is **~2,480** (+3%).
    5. **K3:**
       - **Amazon's $50bn of OpenAI Series C preferred**, entered in Q1 2026 with an AWS commercial
         arrangement, is supplier equity in a customer. It was missing from the count, which is
         now **≥ USD 270bn**. This is a level correction (the commitment predates 12 Oct), not a
         new flow.
       - Capex ÷ OCF was 179% at Amazon in Q3 and ~116% for the five `[A]`, so the signpost's first
         leg is crossed. The debt leg is not measured for the five.
    6. **H2: path kept.**
       - No contract-price decline: Q4-26 is up, and Q1-27 is +3–8%.
       - No LTA floors have been disclosed.
       - CY27 consensus rose again (SK hynix +4.4%, Samsung +4.6% since 29 Oct). That passes
         through the consensus basis, not the path.
       - The Memory cycle section moves the risks (timing earlier, 2029–30 deeper), but no
         independent figure meets a minimum change.
  - **Did any Base path move by more than its trigger? No.** Two recalibration triggers were met
    and re-tested: memory contract-price releases (TrendForce 30 Sep and 20 Oct) and a hyperscaler
    report (Amazon). Nothing moved.
  - **Prices 28/29 Oct → 30 Oct:**
    - SOX −8.5% (−19.1% from its 6 Oct high), Nasdaq-100 −4.8%, Kospi −3.5% (on 30 Oct alone).
    - Compute −5 to −22% (Marvell −22%, AMD −13%, NVIDIA −5%).
    - Memory −3 to −10% (Micron −10.0%, SK hynix −7.1%, Samsung −3.0%).
    - Platforms −14% to 0% (Alphabet −13.9% after its report, Oracle −9.6%, Amazon −5.6%).
    - Software: ServiceNow −30%.
    - Consensus: SK hynix CY27 +4.4%, Samsung +4.6%, Vertiv +6.0%, Microsoft +1.6%, Alphabet
      −2.2%, Amazon +0.9%, ServiceNow −4.0%.

- **Update 5 (29 Oct): one Base point moved (E platforms 2027 .97 → .92); returns fell because
  prices rose.**
  - **Falsification attempt (Base), each hint against its row:**
    1. **E-layer EPS (company check): moved.** Alphabet v2's bottom-up Base is 0.83 of CY27
       consensus in 2027 (0.86 of the layer path), Microsoft v3 0.96 and Amazon v1 0.95. With the
       E-rest row (.97) for Meta and Oracle, the market-cap blend is **.92 in 2027**: gap −0.0505
       at the 28 Oct closes, −0.0475 at post-market weights, .92 either way at the path's two
       decimals. 2028–30 blend 1.04 / 1.16 / 1.27 (gaps −0.03 / −0.01 / −0.00). **Base 2027 .97 →
       .92; 2028–30 kept.**
       - Kept: the Bear (blend .78 / .73 / .81 / .89; gaps ≤ −0.04), the Bull (.98 / 1.18 / 1.35 /
         1.53; gaps ≤ −0.02) and the cash conversion (blend −.23 / −.04 / .17 against −.20 / .00 /
         .20).
       - Effect on the E Base IRR: +0.01 pp. 2027 EPS enters the basket build only through the
         2027 dividend and the retained cash, and these offset at c = −0.20. The implied EPS growth
         for 12% rises from 15% to 17% a year, because it starts from the lower 2027 level.
       - Alphabet's and Microsoft's CY27 consensus is not yet revised after the reports: re-test at
         the next run.
    2. **E2 cloud: Q3 ran ahead; the row is kept.**
       - Q3: Azure +48% (Q4 guide 49–50%), Google Cloud +103% (CY26 ~+89%), OCI +121% (August
         quarter); AWS reports tonight.
       - 2026: the four clouds grow ~+54% `[A]` (AWS at ~+37%) against the row's ~+50%. That is a
         current-year gap of ~4 pp, below the 5 pp rule; re-set with AWS's actuals tomorrow.
       - 2027 Base +38%: the bottom-up company view blends to ~+41% (Azure and AWS at the row,
         Google Cloud +50% on its sourced backlog premium), a 3 pp gap: kept.
       - Run-rate test: the four clouds' Q4-26 exit run-rate is ~18% above the CY26 average. +38%
         therefore implies ~6% sequential growth a quarter in 2027, after 11–16% a quarter in
         2026: a sharp slowdown, but not an implausible one.
       - A per-cloud split of E2 would change a row definition, which is a method change: open
         checkpoint for the next re-baseline.
    3. **K1 capex: kept at 790 / 1,120.** Microsoft calendar 2026 ~170bn (was ~175; third-party
       capacity slipped into 2027); Alphabet 195–205bn unchanged, 2027 to "increase significantly"
       (no figure until February); Oracle FY27 90–95bn. K1 2026 moves < 1%; no first 2027 figure;
       no guide moved > 10%.
    4. **K4 backlog: mixed, ~USD 2,400bn (+2%).** Microsoft commercial RPO flat q/q (677bn,
       bookings −12% y/y); Google Cloud backlog 561bn (+9% q/q, just over 55% within 24 months);
       AWS tonight.
    5. **K3 financing: closer to the Bear.**
       - Alphabet sold **USD 12.4bn of new shares through its ATM in Q3**, more than twice its
         employee withholding (5.4bn): **the platform equity-funding signpost's Bear side is
         crossed.** FCF −9.1bn; capex 121% of OCF.
       - Microsoft: leases not yet commenced 329 → 365bn, 12.9bn of component prepayments, and it
         "will access additional sources of funding". OpenAI paid Microsoft 10.0bn in the quarter
         (10.8% of its revenue).
       - **Correction:** Google's Anthropic commitment is up to USD 30bn (10 paid, 20 of note
         capacity to 2030; the 10bn IPO option reduces that capacity), not 40. Vendor financing is
         therefore ≥ USD 220bn.
    6. **H2 memory: path kept.**
       - SK hynix is ahead at the start: operating profit KRW 83.3tn, ~8% above consensus; Q4 DRAM
         bits +mid-20% q/q; 2027 HBM volumes and prices settled; LTAs with 14 customers on > 50% of

       - Samsung is in line: operating profit 107.4tn as guided; 2027 HBM sold out at target
         prices; 10 multi-year contracts; capex +60% in 2026 and rising in 2027.
       - Both analyses take the H2 path as input, so they are not evidence for its level. Their
         visibility leans against the steepness of the Base 2028 fall (.60); Samsung's capex
         supports its direction. No price floors are disclosed: checkpoint.
       - SK hynix's CY27 consensus rose 8% (KRW 467,761 → 505,086). The layer takes this in through
         the consensus basis, not the path.

- **Update 4 (24 Oct): AI Market BBB kept unchanged; prices fell again, so returns rose.**
  - **22 Oct sell-off:** SOX −6.7% in the day (−18% from its 6 Oct high), Nasdaq −2.6% (its worst
    day since June), CoreWeave −10% (−50% from its high), Meta −7%. Three triggers `[E]`:
    1. **Financing, in NVIDIA's own words:** Jensen Huang (GTC Berlin, 22 Oct) said neocloud
       demand is "off the charts" but **neoclouds lack the working capital to fund their growth**;
       NVIDIA wants lenders to treat its compute as a "financeable asset". This is this baseline's
       main Bear mechanism (financing break, neoclouds first) stated by the largest supplier. It is
       a signpost moving closer, not a crossing: no neocloud has missed a payment.
    2. **Meta's capex and negative free cash flow** (Update 3) revived the "AI bubble" reading.
    3. **Lam Research** (Q1 FY27): record revenue USD 8.22bn (+22% q/q), operating margin above 40%
       for the first time; December guide 8.6bn ± 0.4bn, held back by supply constraints, with
       China (35% of revenue) to fall "meaningfully". Lam −10 to −12%, Applied −6%, ASML −4%.
  - **Falsification attempt:** none of this moves a Base point by its minimum change. Lam's
    record quarter and supply limits support the H3 Base; Meta's capex supports K1; Huang's remark
    raises the Bear's likelihood, which this baseline does not price (25/50/25 is a convention).
  - **Micron v2** (22 Oct): Base 6.3% at $856 (margin of safety −12.6%; fails). Its webinar adds
    supply facts for 2028: 1δ DRAM (+mid-20s% bits per wafer) and G11 NAND ramp in 2H 2027, which
    is evidence *for* the Base 2028 fall.
  - **Anthropic IPO** `[E]`: roadshow reported from the week of 9 Nov, listing before 26 Nov, at up
    to USD 2tn (Series H: 965bn). An IPO above the marks is the financing signpost's Bull side.
  - **Upstream:** Equity, HY and oil baselines renewed or confirmed on 24 Oct with no value
    changed.
  - **Update 4 result (prices 23 Oct):**
    - All six: Base 6.6% (Bear −9.5%, Bull 16.8%).
    - Memory: 12.5%, margin of safety +1.4% (marginal for the third run); E / R 0.338, or 0.114
      with the Bear capped.
    - Compute 6.5%, platforms 6.9%, foundry 6.2%, networking −1.8%, software −2.0%.
    - NVIDIA cleared again at $211.
- **Update 3 (22 Oct): Base paths unchanged; the platform (E) Bear and Bull widened on the company
  analyses; returns a little lower.**
  - **Meta Q3 (21 Oct)** `[F]`: revenue +26% (ad impressions +10%, price per ad +14%); operating
    margin 35% before a USD 9.9bn legal charge; Q3 capex 34.9bn and **free cash flow −2.1bn**; no
    buybacks; 2026 capex narrowed to **135–145bn** (implies ~54bn in Q4); 2027 "continue investing
    aggressively", no number; and a new **non-voting Class C share** (from January 2027) whose
    stated purposes include **capital raising**. It confirms the Base (strong AI-driven ad
    growth, capex still rising) and the financing theme (a second platform preparing equity
    funding after Alphabet).
  - **OpenAI correction** `[E]`: its annualised revenue was **~USD 50bn at end-September**
    (FT/Reuters, 8 Oct), not the ~70bn used here (that is its end-2026 target). OpenAI 2026
    booked revenue ~39bn instead of ~45bn → E1 2026 ~94 instead of ~100 (−6%, below the 10% rule:
    noted, not applied). Anthropic's run-rate includes cloud-partner sales; OpenAI's does not, so
    the two are not like for like.
  - **Amazon** (bottom-up, 21% of the platform basket): Base EPS fits the E path (−0.04 in 2027,
    +0.01 by 2030 with Microsoft and Alphabet; below the 0.05 rule); its Bear and Bull are wider and its free cash
    flow is weaker than Alphabet's. The E-layer Bear, Bull and cash conversion now follow
    Microsoft, Alphabet and Amazon for their 83% of the basket.
  - **Upstream:** World's Base fell to 5.2% (E / R 0.024): the AI baskets (~5.1%) now sit about
    level with World, not below it. USD/NOK Base is 9.41 flat (same relative Bear and Bull moves).
- **Update 2 (17 Oct): the method moved the numbers more than the news.** Four errors in turning
  paths into basket returns were fixed (cap-weighted Base, NOK):

| Step | All six | H1 | H2 | H3 | E | Note |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 16 Oct (prices 14/15 Oct, method v1) | 3.7% | 2.3 | 4.5 | 1.1 | 6.1 | |
| Prices to the 16 Oct close | 4.7% | 3.8 | 4.3 | 3.1 | 6.8 | Hardware −4 to −9% more; Korea +2–4% |
| + calendarised consensus, ASML in EUR | 4.5% | 2.6 | 4.3 | 3.6 | 6.8 | NVIDIA CY27 $15.91 → $15.21 (−4.4%) |
| + retained cash counted once | 6.0% | 5.9 | 12.3 | 5.2 | 5.9 | Was dropped (75% of H1 and 85% of H2 earnings); platforms burn cash |
| + revenue-consistent Bear paths (Base unchanged) | 6.0% | 5.9 | 12.3 | 5.2 | 5.9 | Bear, all six: −5.0% → −9.1% |

  - Retained cash: each layer converts EPS into free cash after growth capex at a rate taken from
    the company analyses (compute ~0.8–0.9, memory 0.6–0.7, foundry ~0.75–0.8, platforms
    −0.2 → +0.2 after Update 3); the retained part accrues to V_T once.
  - Bears: in H1, H3 and H4 the Bear EPS path implied rising margins on falling revenue; the
    NVIDIA and TSMC margin builds give the revenue-consistent paths now used.
- **News in Update 2 (17 Oct; no Base path moved):**
  - **TSMC Q3 call** (open item closed): Q4 guide USD 52.3–54.3bn (+14% q/q), gross margin 65–67%;
    2026 ~+44% in USD; capex 2026–28 "well above" 2023–25; N2 capacity more than doubles in 2027;
    demand above supply; prudent on consumer for 2027 `[F]`. Supports H3 Base 2027.
  - **NVIDIA:** management guides FY28 growth of ~70%, supply-limited through at least the end of
    that year `[E]`. That equals consensus, which is the Bull. Supply commitments USD 279bn,
    "primarily memory", flat at ~88bn a year in FY28–29 `[F]`: consistent with a contracted 2027 and
    a plateau.
  - **Vendor financing is larger than this baseline knew:** NVIDIA guarantees up to **USD 105bn**
    of leases for OpenAI's Ohio campus (8-K, 17 Aug), holds USD 99bn of equity stakes plus 25bn
    committed, and buys back cloud capacity (36bn) `[F]`. With Broadcom's loan of up to 42bn, the
    old signpost "loans to labs > USD 100bn" was already exceeded at the 12 Oct baseline once
    guarantees count. It is redefined as a flow signpost (new commitments, calls, impairments).
    The level confirms that the **financing break is the Bear to watch.**
  - **Mood:** SOX −16% from its 6 Oct high (16 Oct close), Nasdaq-100 −6.5%; Kospi −20% from its
    July high but +2% on 16 Oct.
- **Baseline view (set 12 Oct; 2028–29 capex and compute re-set 11 Nov by the K1 capex cycle):**
- **The market:** AI is the largest capex cycle in the data. The five US hyperscalers spend about
  **USD 790bn in 2026** (+~84%) and the Base takes them to **USD 1.12tn in 2027 (+42%)**, then
  holds a digestion plateau: **1.17tn in 2028 (+4.5%) and 2029 (flat)** while the 2026–27 build
  earns in. Hardware takes the first and largest slice: accelerators grow **+55% in 2027**, then +3%
  and −1%. Memory peaks in 2027 and falls back. Engines grow fastest from a small base:
  frontier-lab revenue **~USD 100bn in 2026 → 360bn by 2029** in Base. Tools grow in AI-native
  products and copilots, while seat-based software slows to high single digits.
- **Durability:** the **monetisation ratio** (lab revenue ÷ hyperscaler capex) rises from 0.04
  (2025) to 0.13 (2026) and 0.31 (2029) in Base, but it rises in every scenario, so it cannot tell a
  sustainable path from an unsustainable one. The **K1 return gauge** can: on the Base paths the AI
  asset base earns ~12% pre-tax in 2026, ~6% in 2027, ~2.5% in 2028 and ~0% in 2029. Capex is
  still three to eight times lab revenue, and it is increasingly financed with debt (new debt equal
  to 32% of capex, LTM) while FCF is near zero for three of five hyperscalers. **The main risk is
  financing, not demand.**
- **Main Bear catalyst:** a **financing break** (a lab falls short of its commitments, Oracle and
  neoclouds renegotiate, credit for AI capex tightens) on top of the equity Bear. **Main Bull
  catalyst:** **agentic adoption** that doubles lab revenue again in 2027 and takes capex to the
  brokers' upside (~USD 1.4tn).

- **2027 Base +42%:** between consensus (+39%) and Goldman (+54%). 2027 is largely contracted:
  backlogs total ~USD 2.5tn (below; Microsoft flat q/q, Google Cloud +9% and AWS +16.5% q/q in Q3), Amazon and
  Microsoft say demand exceeds supply into 2027, and Alphabet expects 2027 capex to "increase

    commenced $364.6bn (Microsoft) and $164.9bn (Amazon); NVIDIA's supply commitments ~$87–88bn a
    year in FY28–29 `[F]`. The plan beyond them is guidance `[E]`. 2027 is the only Base year with a
    contract argument.
- **2028–29 Base: a digestion plateau (K1 capex cycle section).** The 2027 build is contracted.
  After it, Base holds K1 at ~1,170 (2028 +4.5%, 2029 flat) `[A]`:
  - both earlier hyperscaler surges ended in a flat year (2019 +4%, 2023 −2%) `[F]`;
  - E2 slows by 25–28 pp from 2026 to 2028 on its own Base, about as much as AWS before the 2023
    cut;
  - on the Base E-paths the AI asset base earns ~2.5% pre-tax in 2028 and ~0% in 2029 (return
    gauge; coverage of the 10% need 70% and 63%);
  - NVIDIA's supply commitments are flat in FY28–29 `[F]`.

  An absolute cut is the Bear (financing break). Set 11 Nov 2026 (`docs/review-ai-k1-sustainability.md`,
  Option D; history in the Change log).
- **Filings check (11 Nov):** K1 2025 is ~450 by filings (cash capex of the five plus finance
  leases) and 2026 ~815 on nine-month actuals and Q4 guides (Oracle's calendar 2026 ~85, not
  60–70) `[F]`/`[E]`, against ~435 and 790 here. Both gaps are below the 5% rule: noted, not
  applied. The return gauge uses the filed history (165 / 165 / 262 / 450 for 2022–25).
- **Bear:** 2027 builds continue (contracted, power-limited) but H2-27 orders are cut; 2028 falls
  16% as the financing break and the recession bite; 2029 flat `[A]`.

## K1 capex cycle (11 Nov 2026)

**Why this section.** The K1 Base must be the most likely path, not guidance and consensus without
qualification (user, 11 Nov; Option D of `docs/review-ai-k1-sustainability.md`, skill Step 1b).
Guidance sets the current year, contracts the next, and this cycle judgement the years after. The
K1 view is set only here. Evidence, sources (S1–S20) and the full arithmetic are in the review;
`references/k1ret.py` reproduces every gauge figure below.

**The cycle in five lines (11 Nov 2026):**
1. **Where:** the fourth year of the build (the five's capex ~165 → ~262 → ~450 → ~815, 2023–26).
   Capex is 116% of operating cash flow (Q3-26); FCF after dividends and buybacks was −48.8bn in Q3.
   D&A grows 44–90% y/y against revenue 19–39%. GPU rental prices still rise (B200 +27.6% year to
   date), and the term curve is backwardated. Backlog +3% q/q (Microsoft flat).
2. **Return gauge:** on Base K1 and the Base E-paths the AI asset base earns ~12% pre-tax in 2026,
   ~6% in 2027, ~2.5% in 2028 and ~0% in 2029. The revenue needed for 10% is ~860bn (2028) and
   ~1,180bn (2029) against ~600bn and ~740bn delivered: coverage 70% and 63% (51–79% across the
   sensitivities). The gap is locked in by the 2026–27 build; it sets the depth of the eventual
   digestion, not its date.
3. **Past cycles:** returns lagged spending for 4–5 years (telecom) and 10 (shale). Capex turned on
   equity, price, credit or revenue growth; in none of the four cycles on a computed return.
   Hyperscaler surges ended in a flat year (2019 +4%, 2023 −2%).
4. **Judgement against Base:** 2027 contracted (1,120); 2028–29 a plateau (1,170 / 1,170). Risk
   later (AWS "over 40%" in 2027–28, rising rental prices, guidance beats of +10–22% in boom
   years) and deeper (funding of two labs, Meta and Oracle carrying the growth, siting).
5. **Price:** the compute basket earns k at 1.14× its Base 2028–30 path, the platform basket at
   1.10×. NVIDIA's own k-path sits ~0.86× the Base (its v1.3); TSMC needs 1.07× its H3 path (v1.2).
   The compute price still assumes no digestion.

### 1. Where is the cycle?

| Signal | Now (dated) | Reading | Source |
|---|---|---|---|
| Capex growth; capex ÷ OCF | The five: ~+72% (2025), ~+81% (2026E on 9M actuals and Q4 guides) `[F]`/`[A]`. Capex ÷ OCF 116% in Q3-26, 95% TTM; Amazon 182%, Oracle 123%, Alphabet 121% (Q3) `[F]` | Spending exceeds internal cash: the plan depends on markets | Filings via Alpha Vantage (review S3) |
| FCF after dividends and buybacks | The five −66.7bn TTM, **−48.8bn in Q3-26 alone** `[F]`/`[A]` | A funding need of ~$200bn a year at the Q3 pace `[A]` | Review S3 |
| Debt, equity and leases raised | Long-term debt end-2025 → 30 Sep 2026: Alphabet 46.5 → 125.1, Amazon 68.8 → 180.9 (face), Meta 58.7 → 80.9; Oracle 100.0 → 117.7 (Aug), BBB− `[F]`/`[E]`. Microsoft 35.4 → 31.1, but leases not yet commenced $364.6bn `[F]`. Equity: Alphabet $49.6bn + $12.4bn ATM, Oracle $20bn `[F]`/`[E]` | Four of five fund externally; Oracle is the weakest | K3 above; review S3, S8, S10 |
| D&A growth against revenue growth | Q3 y/y: AWS D&A +63% vs revenue +39%; Alphabet +49% vs +25%; Microsoft +56% vs +19%; Meta +44% vs +26%; Oracle +90% vs +30% `[F]` | The depreciation wave has started; the gauge has the five's D&A +44% in 2028 against cloud revenue +25% `[A]` | Review S3, S4, S10 |
| Margins | Microsoft Intelligent Cloud gross margin 66% → 62% → 58% (FY24–26); Meta 35% before its legal charge (40% a year earlier); AWS operating margin 38.7%, up `[F]` | Mixed: the fastest builders' margins fall; AWS's not yet | Review S4, S7, S8 |
| GPU rental prices and term curve | H100 $2.75 an hour (27 Jul); B200 **$5.62** (1 Sep, +27.6% year to date); B200 curve backwardated (3-month 5.73, 12-month 5.39) `[E]`; all four clouds "supply constrained" `[E]` | **No turn signal;** the curve expects lower prices next year | Silicon Data (review S15) |
| K4 backlog q/q | +3% (Q3); Microsoft flat, AWS +16.5%, Google Cloud +9% `[F]` | Slowing at Microsoft | K4 above |
| Customer concentration | OpenAI 10.8% of Microsoft's revenue `[F]`; OpenAI 138 + Anthropic ≥ 110 ≈ 43% of AWS's backlog; Anthropic ≥ 121.3 ≈ 22% of Google Cloud's `[F]`/`[A]`; more than half of Oracle's RPO is OpenAI `[E]` | High: the return rests on two equity-funded labs | K3, K4 above |
| Management language on returns | Asymmetric-risk framing (Zuckerberg, Pichai); **no hyperscaler states a return hurdle for AI capex** `[E]`/`[?]` | The plan is not return-gated | Review S17 |
| Suppliers' commitments | NVIDIA supply commitments flat at ~$87–88bn a year in FY28–29 after the FY27 step `[F]` | A plateau after 2027 is what the largest supplier has contracted for | Hardware below |

**Reading:** the funding and depreciation signals are on the Bear side and the pricing signals on
the Bull side; backlog is mixed. Nothing turns 2027. The question is 2028–29.

### 2. Past cycles

| Episode | Run-up | Returns while spending ran ahead | Funding | What turned it | Signal → capex turn | Fall |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| **US telecom and fibre, 1996–2002** | Real communications-equipment investment ~18% a year to Q4 2000 `[E]`; nominal 70.8 (1996) → 123.3 (2000) `[F]` | The industry lost ~$20bn in 2001; capacity prices fell `[E]` | New entrants' equity and debt `[E]` | Equity first (Nasdaq telecom peak 10 Mar 2000), then overcapacity and falling prices, recession `[E]` | ~3 quarters | Nominal 123.3 → 83.7 (2002, −32%); the 2000 level not regained by 2007 `[F]` |
| **US shale, 2010–2016** | Well investment 86.6 (2010) → 169.8 (2014) `[F]` | Negative FCF every year 2010–19, ~$189bn for 34 producers `[E]` | High-yield debt and equity `[E]` | Price (Brent halved from mid-2014), then credit `[E]` | 2–3 quarters | 169.8 → 63.8 (2016, −62%) `[F]` |
| **Hyperscalers, 2017–2019** | The five's capex 42.6 → 68.2 (2018, +60%) `[F]` | High (operating income +16% in 2019) `[F]` | Operating cash flow | Digestion of the 2018 build, memory down-cycle, trade war `[E]` | ~1 year | +60% → **+4%** (2019); no absolute fall `[F]` |
| **Hyperscalers, 2020–2023** | 97.0 → 130.6 → 158.0 (+37%, +35%, +21%) `[F]` | Meta operating income −38% (2022); AWS growth 37% → 13% (2021–23) `[F]` | Operating cash flow | Revenue growth slowed; Meta cut its 2023 guide $34–37bn → $28.1bn `[E]`/`[F]` | Same year | **−2%** (2023; Amazon −17%, Meta −13%) `[F]` |
| **AI build, 2024–** | ~165 (2023) → ~262 → ~450 → ~815 (2026E): +59% / +72% / +81% `[F]`/`[A]` | Segment margins up today; ~0% on the AI asset base by 2029 on Base (gauge) `[A]` | OCF, then debt, equity, leases, vendor financing ≥ $270bn `[F]`/`[E]` | — | — | — |

Sources: FRED `C275RC1A027NBEA`, `C319RC1A027NBEA`; Richmond Fed Economic Quarterly 89/4 (2003);
IEEFA (search excerpt); filings of the five (review S12–S14, S3, S19).

**What the analogues say:**
- Spending ran ahead of returns for years, and funding kept it going. Funding is no evidence of a
  sustainable return; it can delay the turn for a long time.
- The turn came from a signal outside the accounts: equity and then prices (telecom), price and then
  credit (shale), revenue growth (cloud 2023). None turned on a computed return.
- Hyperscaler surges ended in a flat year after 1–3 years of +21% to +60%. The AI build is in its
  fourth year in 2027 at Base +42%.

| Where today differs | Evidence | Direction |
|---|---|---|
| The issuers are cash-rich and investment-grade | OCF TTM $724bn; IG OAS 0.91% (9 Nov) `[F]` | Later and shallower than telecom or shale |
| Contracted backlog | K4 ~2,480; Google Cloud 55% within 24 months; AWS 6.1 years `[F]`. Two labs are ≥ ~17% of it on filed figures, ≥ ~30% with Oracle's OpenAI share `[F]`/`[E]`/`[A]` | Supports 2027 |
| Prices rise, not fall | H100 and B200 rental indices up in 2026 `[E]`; in telecom capacity prices fell before capex peaked | No turn signal yet |
| The customer layer is equity-funded and loss-making | Labs; neoclouds (CoreWeave: no unsecured debt at current yields) `[E]` | Like the new entrants of 2000 |
| Assets wear out in 5–6 years, not 20+ | Filed server lives 5–6 years; Amazon shortened part from 6 to 5 (2025) `[F]` | Idle capacity cannot wait a decade; the return must come within the asset life |

### 3. The return gauge (`references/k1ret.py`)

Method `[A]` unless tagged: the AI asset base is K1 above a non-AI baseline (140 in 2023, +5% a
year, calibrated on the five's D&A), by monthly vintage, with construction in progress counted.
Short-lived 65% (Alphabet's servers and networking share `[F]`), 5.5 years, in service after 2
months; long-lived 35%, 20 years, after 12 months, 5% of it land. Cash cost 40% of AI revenue
(AWS 41%, CoreWeave 38% `[F]`). Required revenue = (10% × average net AI assets + AI
depreciation) ÷ (1 − cash cost). Supply = the Base E-paths: E2 above a 10% trend from 2023, T1
seats × ~$240 a year, and Meta's revenue above a 10% trend (an upper bound). K1 history from
filings (165 / 165 / 262 / 450); flat after 2029.

| Base K1, USD bn `[A]` | 2025 | 2026 | 2027 | 2028 | 2029 | 2030 | 2031 |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| K1 | 450 | 790 | 1,120 | 1,170 | 1,170 | 1,170 | 1,170 |
| AI capex | 296 | 628 | 950 | 991 | 982 | 973 | 963 |
| Cumulative AI capex | 461 | 1,089 | 2,038 | 3,030 | 4,012 | 4,985 | 5,948 |
| AI depreciation | 35 | 88 | 184 | 309 | 438 | 557 | 654 |
| Average net AI assets | 292 | 708 | 1,374 | 2,095 | 2,702 | 3,176 | 3,531 |
| **Revenue needed, r = 10%** | 106 | 265 | 536 | **864** | **1,181** | 1,458 | 1,679 |
| Revenue needed, r = 0% | 58 | 147 | 307 | 515 | 730 | 929 | 1,091 |
| **Supply** (cloud / Copilot / Meta) | 111 (68 / 5 / 38) | 287 (200 / 10 / 76) | 449 (344 / 14 / 92) | **602** (469 / 23 / 110) | **738** (577 / 31 / 130) | 845 | 944 |
| **Coverage of the 10% need** | 104% | 108% | 84% | **70%** | **63%** | 58% | 56% |
| Gap to the 10% need | — | — | −87 | −262 | −443 | −613 | −735 |
| **Pre-tax return on AI net assets** | 10.9% | 11.9% | 6.2% | **2.5%** | **0.2%** | −1.6% | −2.5% |
| The five's D&A (non-AI 93.5 in 2023 + 5% a year, plus AI) | 138 | 196 | 298 | 428 | 563 | 689 | 793 |

| Sensitivity (Base K1) | Coverage 2028 / 2029 | Revenue needed 2029 | Supply 2029 | Pre-tax return 2028 / 2029 |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| **Central** | **70% / 63%** | **1,181** | **738** | **2.5% / 0.2%** |
| r = 8% / 12% | 76% / 68%; 64% / 58% | 1,091 / 1,271 | 738 | unchanged |
| Cash cost 35% / 45% | 75% / 68%; 64% / 57% | 1,090 / 1,288 | 738 | 3.9% / 1.5%; 1.1% / −1.2% |
| Short life 6.0 years | 72% / 65% | 1,140 | 738 | 3.5% / 1.3% |
| **Short life 4.0 years (economic)** | 60% / 56% | 1,323 | 738 | −2.2% / −4.4% |
| Short-lived share 75% | 66% / 59% | 1,245 | 738 | 0.8% / −1.7% |
| **Strict: no Meta uplift** | 57% / 51% | 1,181 | 608 | −0.7% / −2.7% |
| **E2 at the Bull row** (+45% / +32% / +22%) | 79% / 74% | 1,181 | 870 | 4.9% / 3.1% |

| K1 path 2027 / 2028 / 2029 (supply on the Base E-paths) | Coverage 2027 / 2028 / 2029 / 2030 | Pre-tax return 2028 / 2029 / 2030 |
|---|---|---|
| **Base: plateau 1,120 / 1,170 / 1,170** | 84 / 70 / 63 / 58% | 2.5 / 0.2 / −1.6% |
| Taper on guidance and consensus 1,120 / 1,250 / 1,300 | 84 / 69 / 60 / 54% | 2.3 / −0.4 / −2.4% |
| Flat 1,120 / 1,120 / 1,120 | 84 / 70 / 64 / 60% | 2.6 / 0.5 / −1.1% |
| Holds the 2027 coverage by 2029: 1,120 / 650 / 650 | 84 / 77 / 81 / 84% | 4.2 / 4.8 / 5.3% |
| Bear 950 / 800 / 820 | 88 / 81 / 79 / 78% | 5.2 / 4.4 / 3.6% |
| Bull 1,300 / 1,550 / 1,700 | 80 / 61 / 50 / 44% | 0.5 / −2.6 / −5.0% |

**What the gauge says:**
- **The gap is revenue, and it is large.** The supply is a generous attribution, about twice the
  disclosed AI run-rates (Microsoft > $37bn, AWS > $25bn, OCI ~$30bn annualised `[E]`), and still
  covers only 70% and 63% of the 10% need in 2028–29. No case reaches 10% inside the window, not
  even E2 at its Bull row.
- **It shows in the 2027–28 reports:** coverage falls below 100% in 2027, and the five's D&A rises
  ~+50% a year in 2027–28 against revenue growth of ~13% `[A]`.
- **It is locked in by the 2026–27 build** (~1,580bn of AI capex, depreciating through 2031–33).
  K1 after 2027 barely moves 2028–29 returns; holding the 2027 coverage would need K1 at ~650 in
  2028–29, below the Bear. So the gauge cannot set the level without putting Base under the Bear:
  it measures how much of the build is exposed, and so the depth of the eventual digestion. It is
  not a timer.
- **Base K1 assumes the return arrives after 2029:** AI revenue must keep growing well above +12%
  a year after 2029, or the companies accept returns below their cost of capital (after-tax WACC
  ~9–10%, ~11–12.5% pre-tax `[A]`; 10-year Treasury 4.88%, IG OAS 0.91% on 9 Nov `[F]`).
- Cross-check: the three analysed platforms' own D&A paths plus ~65 / 100 / 130 for Meta and Oracle
  `[A]` give 304 / 413 / 504 in 2027–29, within 2–12% of the five's D&A above.

### 4. Judgement against Base

| Year | Base `[A]` | Judgement | Later or higher than Base | Earlier or deeper than Base |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| **2027** | 1,120 (+42%), range 1,050–1,200 | **Contracted: kept** | AWS "over 40%" in 2027–28 (release `[E]`); NVIDIA FY28 ~70%, supply-limited `[E]`; guidance beat the initial guide by +10–22% in 2024–26 `[E]`; Goldman 1,200, upside 1,400 `[E]` | Microsoft's RPO flat, bookings −12% y/y `[F]`; capex 116% of OCF and FCF after returns −48.8bn in Q3 `[F]`; the K1 growth after 2026 rests on Meta and Oracle, the two names with no analysis and the weakest return evidence |
| **2028** | **1,170 (+4.5%)**, range 1,000–1,350 | **A digestion year, at the top of the past range** (+4%, −2%) | Backlog due in 2028 (Google Cloud 55% within 24 months; AWS 6.1 years); NVIDIA's commitments flat, not falling `[F]`; rental prices still rising `[E]`; IG spreads 0.91%; the labs are funded through 2027 | Return ~2.5% pre-tax (coverage 70%); D&A +44% against cloud revenue +25% `[A]`; two equity-funded labs ≥ ~17–30% of backlog; US siting policy (Texas, Georgia, Ohio) `[E]` |
| **2029** | **1,170 (0%)**, range 950–1,400 | **Flat:** no backlog evidence reaches it, and the return gap is at its widest | E2 still +18% on Base; the three analysed platforms with Meta and Oracle at the same growth give ~1,175 bottom-up `[A]` | Coverage 63%, return ~0%; −4.4% with a four-year economic life; 51% coverage on strict attribution |
| **Overall** | 790 / 1,120 / 1,170 / 1,170 | **Base kept as set on 11 Nov.** A point moves only by the minimum change (5% of the capex level, ~58bn). An absolute cut is the Bear's financing break (950 / 800 / 820), and nothing signals it yet: prices rise, IG spreads are tight and the labs are funded through 2027. 25/50/25 remains a convention, not a probability | | |

### 5. What the price implies

Basket method (each layer's Base exit P/E, payout and cash conversion; 6 Nov prices, 7 Nov
consensus; Base FX). 2027 is held at Base, and 2028–30 is scaled by a uniform multiplier;
`references/impl.py H1` and `impl.py E` reproduce the basket rows.

| Basket or name | P/E CY27 | Base IRR (NOK) | 2028–30 × Base for **k = 12%** (path) | for **0%** (path) | Source |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| **H1 compute basket** | 13.71 | 7.5% | **1.14** (1.04 / 1.00 / .99) | 0.79 (.72 / .70 / .69) | `impl.py H1` |
| **E platform basket** | 20.95 | 8.6% | **1.10** (1.18 / 1.29 / 1.40) | 0.78 (.83 / .91 / .99) | `impl.py E` |
| NVIDIA, own build (v1.3, $168.89) | — | — | k-path .76 / .78 / .75 of CY27 consensus = **~0.86×** the Base `[A]` | — | `docs/nvda-analysis.md`; restated at its 17 Nov run |
| TSMC, own build (v1.2, $396.48) | — | — | **1.07×** its H3 path (unchanged) | 0.76× | `docs/tsm-analysis.md` |

**Reading:** the compute basket's price needs H1 EPS to hold at about the CY27 consensus level
through 2030 (1.04 / 1.00 / .99), i.e. no digestion; on Base it digests to .91 / .88 / .87.
NVIDIA's own build is cheaper: its k-path sits ~14% below Base. The platforms need ~10% more than
Base 2028–30 (their path grows; it does not depend on K1 except through cash).

| Late Jan–early Feb 2027 | First 2027 capex figures: Microsoft (~27 Jan), Meta (~28 Jan), Alphabet (~2 Feb), Amazon (~4 Feb) | K1 2027 1,050–1,250 | > 1,300, or "continued strong growth" for 2028 from three of five | < 1,000, or a platform guiding next-year capex flat or down: toward 1,120 flat, or the Bear if funding breaks |
| Each quarter (Q4 reports) | FCF after shareholder returns (five) | Between −50bn and 0 a quarter | Positive | Below −50bn a quarter twice with E2 growth slowing |

| Row, Bear / **Base** / Bull | 2026 | 2027 | 2028 | 2029 |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| **H1 Compute** (accelerators + AI networking silicon), USD bn | **~470** `[A]` (440–490) | 585 / **730** / 820 | 470 / **750** / 985 | 490 / **745** / 1,080 |
| Growth y/y | | +25% / **+55%** / +75% | −20% / **+3%** / +20% | +5% / **−1%** / +10% |
| **H2 Memory** revenue, index (2026 average = 100; Q4-26 run-rate ≈ 130) | 100 | 100 / **138** / 150 (was 90 / 115 / 125) | 72 / **118** / 133 (was 58 / 92 / 119) | 67 / **103** / 118 (was 58 / 78 / 101) |
| **H3 Foundry, packaging, equipment**, index (2026 average = 100; TSMC Q4-26 run-rate ≈ 121) | 100 | 110 / **125** / 132 | 99 / **140** / 156 | 104 / **151** / 175 |
| **H4 Networking and DC infrastructure**, index | 100 | 110 / **125** / 135 | 88 / **129** / 159 | 88 / **130** / 175 |

- **K1 re-set carried through (11 Nov, skill 2026-11-11.2, Step 1b) `[A]`:** K1 Base 2028–29 is
  1,170 / 1,170 (K1 capex cycle section). The hardware rows set from K1 follow it by their own
  rules and the minimum change; `references/model.py` (switch `K1D`) prints the derivation.
  - **H1 compute: 750 / 745** (748.8 / 742.5 unrounded). Base keeps the non-K1 share (Step 2), so
    H1 holds its ratio to K1: 0.595 / 0.652 / 0.641 / 0.637 in 2026–29. In the compute-share build
    of the H1 review (K1-linked H1 = K1 × the 2026 chip intensity 0.361 × a content uplift of 1.10
    in 2028–29, non-K1 share 38.0% / 37.5%) this is the same figure. The change (−6.4% / −9.7%)
    is above the 5% rule.
  - **H4: 129 / 130** (129.2 / 129.6). H4's Base growth tracked K1's in 2028–29 in every scenario,
    so it takes the same ratio to K1 as H1. The 2028 growth moves 7 pp (rule 5 pp).
  - **H3: kept (125 / 140 / 151).** H3 reads K1 only through H1. At the 20–30% pass-through used
    for TSMC (AI accelerators "high-teens" % of 2025 revenue, growing fastest `[E]`) the index
    would be 138.2–137.3 / 148.0–146.5: growth −1.4 to −2.2 pp in 2028, −0.8 to −1.2 pp in 2029,
    and EPS −0.02 to −0.03 (2028) and −0.03 to −0.04 (2029–30). All are below the 5 pp and 0.05
    rules, so they are noted, not applied.
  - **H2: kept.** Its method reads K1 only through the Memory cycle section's demand channel, and
    its Base turn in 2028 already assumes AI capex digestion after the contracted 2027 build (see
    the note in that section).
  - **H5 power:** a constraint row with no path. A flat K1 in 2028–29 means fewer new gigawatts
    needed in those years; the siting risk to 2028 stays a signpost.
- **H1 2026 build `[A]`:** NVIDIA data center FY27 ≈ CY26: Q1 ~75 (back-solved from +18% q/q),
  Q2 **89.0** `[F]`, Q3 ~100 (from the 108.0 total guide `[F]`), Q4 ~112 → ~376; Broadcom AI semis
  FY26 ~58 `[E]` (Q3 16.7 `[E]`, Q4 guide 21.7 `[E]`); AMD Instinct ~12 `[A]` (data center
  segment 6.7 in Q2 incl. EPYC `[E]`); custom and other ~20 `[A]`. NVIDIA Q1 data center 75.3
  `[F]` (10-Q), as back-solved.
- **H1 buyers outside K1 (Base assumption; 11 Nov):** H1 is set from K1, but 30–46% of H1 2026 (~140–215bn
  of ~470; central ~185) is bought outside the five platforms' capex: AI clouds and neoclouds,
  xAI, the labs' own and leased builds, sovereign and enterprise `[A]`. NVIDIA's ACIE segment was
  45% of its Q2 FY27 data center `[F]`; the sizing is in `docs/review-ai-h1-non-k1-funding.md`.
  - Base 730 / 750 / 745 keeps that share about constant: non-K1 H1 ~+60% in 2027 (range +43% to
    +76%), then ~285 and ~278 (central), digesting with K1 `[A]`. **Base therefore assumes these
    buyers stay funded at their current share.**
  - Their 2027 funding is mostly raised: labs' equity and IPOs, SpaceX's IPO and notes. Neocloud
    debt is still to be raised.
  - NVIDIA's direct support is ~5–8% of non-K1 H1 `[A]`.
  - A shortfall is the financing-break Bear, which already holds non-K1 H1 −25 to −30% in 2027 and
    about −46% and −41% in 2028–29 (central) against Base `[A]`.
  - Signpost: "Non-K1 compute funding".
- **NVIDIA data (17 Oct, `docs/nvda-analysis.md`) `[F]` unless noted:**
  - Q2 FY27 revenue by platform: Hyperscale 48.7bn (+102% y/y), AI Clouds, Industrial and
    Enterprise 40.3bn (+138%), Edge 7.2bn. Q3 guide 108.0bn ± 2%, gross margin 74.0%; report
    **17 Nov**.
  - Management: FY28 growth **~70%**, supply-limited "through at least the end of that year";
    Rubin ~20% of Q3 data center revenue and ~USD 40bn of NVIDIA revenue per GW (25bn for
    Blackwell) `[E]`. This equals consensus, i.e. the H1 Bull; it is guidance and does not move
    the Base (+55% in 2027).
  - Working capital: receivables +24.6bn in H1 on extended payment terms (DSO 60 from 45);
    inventory 31.6bn ahead of Rubin.
  - Supply commitments 279bn (92bn rest of FY27, 87bn FY28, 88bn FY29): flat after the 2027 step,
    which fits a contracted 2027 and a plateau; mostly memory, which supports H2 volumes in 2027–28.
  - **H1 Bear path reconciled:** on the layer's Bear revenue (585 / 470 / 490) the old Bear EPS
    (.70 / .60 / .70 / .75) needed NVIDIA's net margin to rise from 54% to 64% while revenue fell.
    At gross margin 68–72% and opex USD 42–47bn, NVIDIA's Bear is **.68 / .50 / .53 / .57**; it is
    76% of the basket's CY27 earnings, so the layer Bear now uses it.
- **H1 2027 Base +55%:** below the supply-limited ~70% NVIDIA cites for FY28 `[E]` and consensus
  CY27 EPS growth of +74% for the basket `[F]`; above K1's +42% because the compute share of capex
  rises (Microsoft: two-thirds short-lived assets `[E]`) and memory costs raise system prices.
  Broadcom's FY27 AI figure of ~USD 115bn (double) is from a transcript summary only `[?]`.
- **H2:** Q4 2026 DRAM contract prices +15–20% q/q incl. HBM; Q1 2027 +3–8% (server, PC);
  2027 DRAM and HBM output reported sold out `[E]` (TrendForce via Goldman; DigiTimes). Base: 2027
  up on price, 2028–29 down as new capacity arrives (2017–19 analogue) `[A]`.
- **H3:** TSMC guided 2026 growth above 40% in USD and raised 2026 capex to USD 60–64bn; CoWoS
  sold out into 2027 `[E]`.

## H2 Memory cycle (1 Nov 2026) — the lead section for the Samsung, SK hynix and IQQK analyses

**Why this section.** The user set a rule on 1 Nov 2026, after the review of the KOSPI analysis
(`docs/review-kospi-analysis.md`, section 5). Opportunistic cyclical equity positions in memory
(IQQK, Samsung, SK hynix) lead with **the cycle**: where it is, what can turn it, and how likely
and when. Only then comes what the price implies. E / R against high yield and precise IRRs are
not the gate for these positions:
- HY's R is ~9 pp and a memory share's 40–60 pp, so an equity needs about five times HY's excess
  return to tie.
- The IRR is mostly an echo of one assumption, the 2030 mid-cycle.

The memory view is set only here (CLAUDE.md). `docs/skhynix-analysis.md`,
`docs/samsung-analysis.md` and `docs/kospi-analysis.md` lead with this section, and
`docs/mu-analysis.md` may. **The H2 paths are unchanged:** this section is an assessment, not a
re-baseline.

**K1 re-set (11 Nov):** the K1 Base now holds a digestion plateau in 2028–29 (1,170 / 1,170; K1
capex cycle section). That is the AI capex digestion after the contracted 2027 build which this
section's Base turn in 2028 already assumes, so the H2 paths and the judgement in section 4 are
unchanged. The K1 figures in sections 3 and 4 are the current Base; the depth check in section 3
now has memory tracking AI capex at ~−14% per bit in 2028, still shallower than the Base's ~−30%.

**The cycle in five lines (1 Nov 2026):**
1. **Where:** late upcycle. Contract prices still rise, but q/q growth has halved twice since Q2-26
   (~80% → ~40% → ~20%; Q1-27 +3–8%). Mobile DRAM and NAND outside enterprise SSD are about flat.
   Supplier inventory is under two weeks, and margins are records. The shares peaked in June. The
   Korean ones have already fallen as far as in the full 2018–19 and 2021–22 downturns; Micron has
   fallen less.
2. **What turns it:** supply from 2H-27 into 2028 meets AI capex digestion after the contracted
   2027 build, and a consumer market that is already shrinking at today's prices. The supply comes
   from new fabs, the 1δ / G11 nodes, CXMT's +31% in wafers, and HBM wafers returning to DRAM if
   HBM growth slows. The LTAs soften the fall for the leaders but cap the rise, and SK hynix and
   Samsung disclose no floors.
3. **Likelihood and timing against Base (turn in 2028): Base kept.**
   - Timing: about Base, with the risk 1–2 quarters earlier for NAND and commodity DRAM.
   - 2028 depth: Base or shallower for the leaders.
   - 2029–30: Base or deeper.
4. **What the price implies:** the memory basket earns k at 0.97× the Base 2028–30 path and 0% at
   0.65×. **The price discounts the Base turn, not an earlier or deeper one.** SK hynix prices a
   path 23% below Base, Samsung 14% below, and Micron 43% above.
   - **7 Nov re-mark (6 Nov closes):** the basket earns k at **0.75×** Base and 0% at 0.49× (0.80×
     and 0.52× on 5 Nov). **The price now discounts a turn about a quarter deeper or earlier than
     Base.** SK hynix prices 43% below Base, Samsung 32% below, and Micron about Base (1.08×).
     Section 5 has the detail.
5. **Signposts:**
   - the contract prints (end-Nov, end-Dec) and TrendForce's Q1-27 outlook;
   - LTA ranges or floors in the Q3 business reports (~14–16 Nov);
   - NVIDIA (17 Nov), Micron FQ1 (16 Dec) and Samsung's Q4 preliminary (~7 Jan);
   - the 2027 memory capex figures (late Jan);
   - foreign flows in Kospi.

### 1. Where is the cycle?

TrendForce names three reasons: a higher base, weaker end-market purchasing power, and **LTA price ceilings** taking effect | TrendForce 30 Sep and 20 Oct `[E]`; Q1-26 via secondary `[E]`; Q1-27: TrendForce via Goldman `[E]` |

They also cap the rise: the ceilings already bite in Q4-26 | Company releases and calls `[F]`/`[E]` |

CY27 revenue over CY26: SK hynix +58%, Samsung group +40%, Micron ~+50%. **7 Nov:** SK hynix 529,348 (+0.4%), Samsung 77,324 (+2.2%), Micron 191.8 | Rising | Analysts have not called a peak; no rating downgrades in October | Yahoo 1 and 7 Nov `[F]`; Benzinga via BigGo (30 Oct) `[E]` |

**Reading:** late upcycle.
- Contract prices still rise into Q1-27, and the earnings path in 2027 is mostly contracted. The
  Base 2027 index of 138 is only ~6% above the Q4-26 run-rate of ~130, so the Base already assumes
  a plateau in 2027.
- But the momentum peaked in Q2-26. The weakest segments (mobile DRAM, client and mobile NAND) are
  flat, and the margins are far above any earlier peak.
- In past cycles this configuration came 1–4 quarters before the first contract-price decline: a
  share-price peak, decelerating contract growth, record margins and a capex step-up.

### 2. Past cycles: lead times

| | 2016–19 | 2020–23 | This cycle (2025–) |
|---|---|---|---|
| Capex step-up | Samsung semiconductor capex ~KRW 13tn (2016) → 27.3tn (2017); SK hynix ~17tn in 2018 | Samsung semiconductor ~33tn (2020) → 43.6tn (2021); SK hynix ~19tn in 2022 | **Samsung 52.7 → 84.4tn (2026, +60%)**, higher in 2027. SK hynix 2027 "significantly" higher. Micron gross capex $15.9bn (FY25) → $30.7bn (FY26) → ~$25bn in 1H FY27 alone. The three makers ~$146bn in 2027 `[E]`. CXMT ~320k → ~420k wafers a month by 2027 `[E]` |
| First q/q contract-price fall | **Q4-18** (DRAM revenue −18.3% q/q). In 2019 DRAM quotes fell > 50% and DRAM revenue 37.5% | **Q4-21** (−3–8%, "due to rising level of client inventory"), after +3–8% in Q3-21; steep falls from Q3-22 | **Not yet:** Q4-26 +10–15%, Q1-27 +3–8% |
| Capex step-up → first price fall | ~6–7 quarters (supply-led) | 0–3 quarters (demand-led) | The step began in 1H-26. The 2017 lag puts the first fall in Q3-27 to Q1-28; new DRAM fabs ramp in 2H-27 with "substantial output" in 2028 (TrendForce, Jul) |
| Share-price peak | Samsung 1 Nov 2017; SK hynix 23 May 2018; Micron 29 May 2018 | Samsung 11 Jan 2021; SK hynix 25 Feb 2021; Micron 14 Jan 2022 | Samsung 18 Jun 2026; SK hynix 22 Jun; Micron 25 Jun |
| Earnings peak | Q3-18: SK hynix operating profit 6.47tn, a record, reported "as chip outlook dims"; Micron FQ4-18 (to Aug 2018): operating income $4.38bn, gross margin 61.0% | SK hynix Q4-21 and Q2-22 (~4.2tn each); Samsung DS Q3-21 and Q2-22; Micron FQ4-21 and FQ3-22 (to May 2022; $3.0bn) | **Not yet:** CY27 consensus is 47–64% above CY26, and Micron guides sequential revenue growth in every quarter of FY27 |
| Share peak → earnings peak | 3 months (Micron), 4 (SK hynix), 11 (Samsung) | 4 months (Micron), 8–17 (Samsung, SK hynix) | 4+ months so far. The past range puts the earnings peak between Sep 2026 and Nov 2027 |
| Peak-to-trough share fall | Samsung −35%, SK hynix −39%, Micron −54% | Samsung −42%, SK hynix −49%, Micron −50% | So far: Samsung −38%, SK hynix −51%, Micron −27% (30 Oct); **Samsung −48%, SK hynix −61%, Micron −43% (6 Nov)** |
| Share trough → earnings trough | Dec 2018–Jan 2019 → Q4-19 (SK hynix ~0.24tn) and FQ1-20 (Micron $0.52bn): ~11 months | Sep–Dec 2022 → Q1-23 (SK hynix −3.4tn; Micron FQ2-23 −$2.3bn): 3–6 months | — |
| What turned it | Supply (the 2017 capex, node bits), plus a hyperscaler pause and the US–China trade war in late 2018 | Demand: PC and phone digestion after COVID, client inventory, rate rises | — |

Sources: share prices are Yahoo daily closes `[F]`; Micron's quarterly figures come from Alpha
Vantage `[F]`. The rest is `[E]`: SK hynix's 2018 figures via press (Reuters, Investing.com); the
2019 DRAM revenue (Gartner) and 2018–19 quotes (TrendForce) via press; TrendForce of 22 Sep 2021;
and the Korean capex and later operating-profit figures as reported at the time (not re-read in
this run).

**What the analogues say:**
- **Shares lead.** In both past cycles the shares peaked 3–17 months before earnings and fell
  35–54%. This time they peaked in June. Korea has already fallen as far as in a full downturn, so
  the market is pricing the turn ahead of the data, as it did before.
- **Prices turn about one quarter after q/q growth reaches low single digits.** In 2021 +3–8% was
  followed by −3–8%; in 2018 prices flattened through Q3 and fell in Q4.
  - On that pattern Q2-27, after Q1-27's +3–8%, would bring the first decline. That is earlier than
    the Base's annual 2028 fall.
  - Two things differ this time, and both slow a slide: the LTAs (ranges with ceilings on > 50–75%
    of the leaders' output) and HBM's claim on wafers.
- **The supply lag points to 2028.** The capex lag of 2017–18 (6–7 quarters) and the fab timetable
  (new DRAM fabs ramp in 2H-27) both put the supply-driven turn in 2028.

### 3. What can turn it

| Channel | Evidence | Direction and timing |
|---|---|---|
| **Supply: the capex wave** | Samsung 84.4tn in 2026 (+60%), higher in 2027: P4 lower line Nov 2026, P5 in full operation 2028, Taylor 2027. SK hynix: M15X at full capacity by end-2026, Yongin fab 1 Feb 2027 (full operation in 2027), M17 Dec 2028, Y2 Jun 2029; 2027 capex "significantly" higher. Micron: ID1 and Tongluo mid-2027, ID2 and Japan late 2028, Singapore NAND 2H-28 `[F]`/`[E]` | Bits from 2H-27, mostly in 2028 and 2029. The 2028–29 wave is larger than 2017–19's in dollars: Micron's capex about doubles two years running (FY27 inferred from the ~$25bn 1H guide `[A]`), and Samsung's rises 60% on a larger base |
| **Supply: node bits** | Micron's 1δ DRAM (+mid-20s% bits per wafer) and G11 NAND (~2× density) ramp in 2H-27. Micron sees industry DRAM bits +low-20s% in 2027 and 2028, and NAND +mid-20s% `[F]` | 2028: bits without new cleanrooms. It is in the Base |
| **Supply: CXMT and China** | ~8% of DRAM revenue in Q1-26; ~320k → ~420k wafers a month by 2027 (+31%), with an aim of 500k; IPO $8.6bn (Jul). Counterpoint's base case is 11% of DRAM bits by 2028, Nomura's 18%. 4F² commercial "after 2028" (industry sources) `[E]` | 2027–28, in commodity DRAM first (Chinese PCs and phones). Export controls are a tail |
| **Supply: HBM → DRAM** | HBM takes ~3× the wafer of DDR5 per bit (Micron, 2024 calls `[E]`). If HBM growth slows, or stacks shrink (8-Hi in place of 12-Hi would cut HBM per GPU by ~33%; Commercial Times via TrendForce `[E]`), the released wafers give ~3 DDR5 bits for each HBM bit given up | The swing factor for 2028–29. The 2028 HBM negotiations (around mid-2027) are the signpost |
| Supply, slower (against) | Capex buys shells and cleanrooms first. Samsung: "higher capex does not add bits in proportion" (more metal layers in HBM4E and HBM5). SK hynix stages its investment ("scale and timing flexible") `[F]`/`[E]` | Delays the bits, which supports the Base's 2028 rather than 2027 |
| **Demand: AI capex digestion** | K1 Base +42% in 2027, then a plateau: +4.5% (2028) and flat (2029) (K1 capex cycle section). Memory's share of hyperscaler capex rises from ~35% (2026) to ~48% (2027) on CLSA's estimate `[E]`, inside budgets that already exceed operating cash flow (the five ~116% in Q3 `[A]`). **Value test** `[A]`: TrendForce's 2027 memory market (> USD 1.28tn; DRAM 903bn, +46%) `[E]` exceeds the five hyperscalers' Base 2027 capex (1,120bn) | 2028 |
| **Demand: US siting policy (new 5 Nov)** | Governors who pledged data-center pauses won Texas (~27% of planned US GW), Georgia (~7%) and Ohio (~5%); Texas has paused new loads ≥ 75 MW since 3 Aug (audit due 10 Dec). No law, cancellation or guidance change yet (Drivers, "US midterms") `[E]` | 2028: fewer new US sites energised means fewer accelerators and less HBM and server DRAM installed that year; the 2027 build and the LTAs are not hit. It leans to the earlier or deeper side for 2028 demand, with no figure yet |
| **Demand: AI lab financing** | The platform equity-funding signpost is crossed (Alphabet's ATM). Vendor financing is ≥ USD 270bn. Huang (22 Oct): neoclouds lack the working capital to fund their growth | Bear channel: it hits HBM and server DRAM in the year after (2028) |
| **Demand: phones and PCs at high prices** | Smartphones −12.9% in 2026 (IDC, Feb) and Q3-26 −20% y/y (Counterpoint, preliminary). PCs −11.3% (IDC, Feb). TrendForce (Jul) expects both to fall again in 2027; PC makers cut SSD capacity per unit `[E]` | The non-AI floor is weaker than in 2018, so a pause in AI goes deeper |
| **Demand: customer inventory** | Module houses and phone makers hold stock (TrendForce). Hyperscaler stock built during the shortage is not measured `[?]` | The 2021 trigger. It would show first as flat or falling mobile and PC contracts |
| Demand: easing statements | Broadcom's CEO (12 Oct): wafer and memory shortages easing; "capital" is now the constraint `[E]`. Against: Micron and Samsung say "tight through 2028", SK hynix "demand above supply through 2030" (guidance `[E]`); TrendForce (Jul): the DRAM gap widens in 2027 | Mixed; the suppliers' guidance sits on the Bull side |
| **Contracts: ranges renegotiated** | SK hynix sets prices "within a price range agreed upon with customers" that "reflects market conditions": a range follows the market. The ceilings already bind in Q4-26 (TrendForce) | Renegotiated ranges are the contract channel of the Bear |
| **Contracts: floors** | Micron: floors on > 35% of revenue through 2030 at margins above prior-cycle peaks; $32bn of customer commitments ($12.75bn in cash deposits). SK hynix: deposits among its safeguards, amounts not disclosed. Samsung: none disclosed. The Q3 business reports (~14 Nov Samsung, ~16 Nov SK hynix) may show more `[F]`/`[?]` | A disclosed floor near today's prices would make 2028 shallower than Base; ranges without floors leave the Base as it is |

**Depth check (value per bit)** `[A]`:
- If memory revenue grows in 2028 only with AI capex (+4.5% on the K1 Base) while DRAM bits grow
  ~22%, the price per bit falls ~14%.
- The Base's 2028 revenue index (138 → 118, −14%) implies ~−30% per bit.
- In 2019 quotes fell > 50% and revenue 37.5% `[E]`.

So the Base's 2028 depth sits between a market that tracks AI capex and a 2019 replay. The EPS fall
(.90 → .60, −33%) is steeper than the revenue fall because the margins start from records.

### 4. How likely, and when: the judgement against Base

| Dimension | Base (EPS path unchanged since 12 Oct) | Judgement | For the judgement | Against it |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| **Timing of the turn** | 2027 up on price but flat from the Q4-26 run-rate (index 138 against ~130); EPS .90 of CY27 consensus; the fall in 2028 | **About Base; risk 1–2 quarters earlier.** The first q/q declines may come in 2H-27 for NAND and commodity and mobile DRAM. HBM and LTA server DRAM follow in 2028 | q/q growth halved twice; mobile 0–5% and NAND flat already; the 2021 pattern; customer stock at the edges; UBS's NAND peak in CQ3-27; TrendForce's looser NAND in 2H-27; IDC sees prices "stabilise by mid-2027" | LTAs on > 50–75% of output (the ceilings work both ways); 2027 HBM settled; supplier stock < 2 weeks; TrendForce: DRAM up "through 2027"; new fabs only from 2H-27 |
| **Depth in 2028** | EPS .60 (−33%); revenue −14% (≈ −30% per bit) | **Base or shallower for the three leaders** | Micron's floors and > 75% committed; settled HBM; capacity staged in shells; AI inference demand | No floors at SK hynix or Samsung; ranges follow the market; a weaker consumer floor than in 2018 |
| **Depth 2029–30 (mid-cycle)** | .40 / .35 (2030 ≈ 2.7× SK hynix's 2025 EPS and ~4× Samsung's 2025 net income: the AI-era structural assumption) | **Base or deeper: the main risk** | The 2028–29 supply wave (Yongin, P5, ID2, Japan, M17, Y2, CXMT toward 500k, 1δ, G11) lands while AI capex is flat (2029, K1 Base); HBM wafers can return to DRAM | More AI content per system (HBM per GPU, inference); oligopoly discipline and LTAs; 1δ cost per bit supports margins |
| **Overall** | | **Base kept.** The evidence moves the timing risk slightly earlier and the 2029–30 risk deeper, and holds the 2028 point. No independent figure (a floor, a price decline, a capex figure) meets a minimum change. 25/50/25 remains a convention, not a probability | | |

### 5. What the price implies

Basket method: Base exit P/E 10, payout 15%, cash conversion .70 / .65 / .60, Base FX. Prices
are the 30 Oct closes and consensus is from 1 Nov; `references/impl.py` reproduces every figure.
2027 is held at the Base .90, and 2028–30 is scaled by a uniform multiplier on the Base
.60 / .40 / .35:

| | P/E CY27 | Base IRR (NOK) | 2028–30 × Base for **k = 12%** (path) | for **0%** (path) | Flat 2028–30 level for k / 0% |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| **Memory basket** | 3.29 | 12.9% | **0.97** (.58 / .39 / .34) | **0.65** (.39 / .26 / .23) | .36 / .24 |
| SK hynix | 2.69 | 20.7% | 0.77 (.46 / .31 / .27) | 0.50 (.30 / .20 / .17) | .28 / .18 |
| Samsung | 2.98 | 16.7% | 0.86 (.52 / .35 / .30) | 0.57 (.34 / .23 / .20) | .32 / .21 |
| Micron | 4.61 | 1.0% | 1.43 (.86 / .57 / .50) | 0.96 (.58 / .39 / .34) | .53 / .35 |

Turn-date and depth paths for the basket (EPS 2027 / 28 / 29 / 2030, CY27 consensus = 1):

| Path | EPS | IRR, exit P/E 10 | IRR, exit P/E 8 |
|---|---|---|---|
| **Base: turn in 2028 (2017–19 analogue)** | .90 / .60 / .40 / .35 | **12.9%** | 7.1% |
| Earlier turn: prices fall from 2H-27 | .80 / .45 / .35 / .35 | 11.2% | 5.4% |
| Later turn: prices hold through 2028, fall in 2029 | .95 / .85 / .55 / .40 | 18.7% | 12.8% |
| Shallower: LTA floors hold, higher mid-cycle | .90 / .70 / .55 / .45 | 21.1% | 14.8% |
| Deeper: a 2019-depth fall, pre-AI-style mid-cycle | .90 / .45 / .30 / .25 | 3.0% | −2.0% |
| Bull path | 1.00 / .80 / .55 / .45 | 22.1% | 15.9% |
| Bear path, at the Base exit P/E | .60 / .30 / .25 / .25 | −0.3% | −5.6% |

**Reading:**
- **The basket price discounts the Base path almost exactly.** The market prices the Base turn,
  not an earlier or deeper one. For the basket to beat k, the turn must be later or shallower than
  Base. To lose money, 2028–30 must be ~35% below Base: about the Bear's depth after a Base 2027.
- **The Korean names price a deeper turn than Base** (SK hynix 23% below it, Samsung 14% below).
  **Micron prices a shallower one** (43% above it: 2028 at .86, close to management's "tight
  through 2028").
- **Timing is mostly priced; depth is not.** A turn half a year earlier costs ~2 pp and a year
  later adds ~6 pp. The mid-cycle and the exit multiple dominate: at an exit P/E of 8 the basket
  needs 1.17× Base for k, at 11 it needs 0.90×.
- **Basis caveat:** the multipliers are on today's consensus, and SK hynix's CY27 is +12.9% since
  16 Oct. Under the layer method a rise in peak-year consensus lifts 2028–30 one for one. Whether
  the mid-cycle should be anchored to peak-year consensus is an open method point (checkpoint 4).
  The basket's CY27 consensus is 6.4% higher than on 16 Oct (earnings-weighted). With 2028–30
  anchored on the 16 Oct basis, the Base would return 10.9% instead of 12.9%, and the price would
  have to be ~5% lower for the same return `[A]`.
- **The company analyses imply a deeper discount still.** They carry net cash, the fast cash
  return and the preference line, which this method leaves out. SK hynix (21.1%) and Samsung
  (23.3%) failed k only at 2030 EPS ×0.6 and ×0.5 in their v2 runs. They should state their own
  "price implies x× Base 2028–30" in the same form.

### 6. Signposts with dates

| Date | Event | Confirms Base | Earlier or deeper turn | Later or shallower turn |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| ~30 Nov, ~31 Dec | DRAMeXchange / TrendForce monthly contract prints (DDR4 and DDR5 PC, NAND MLC) | DRAM still up m/m; NAND flat | DRAM flat or down m/m; NAND down; spot below contract | DRAM up > 5% m/m; NAND rising again |
| Late Dec–early Jan | TrendForce Q1-27 contract outlook | Blended +0–8% | Conventional DRAM flat or negative; mobile DRAM and NAND down | > +10%; 2H-27 still rising |
| ~14 Nov (Samsung, DART), ~16 Nov (SK hynix, 6-K) | Q3 business reports: LTA terms, deposits, contract liabilities | Ranges without floors | Ranges lowered, renegotiation language, contract liabilities falling | Floors or take-or-pay terms disclosed; large deposits |
| 17 Nov | NVIDIA Q3 FY27: Q4 guide, supply commitments (279bn, "primarily memory"), HBM per GPU | Commitments flat to up; guide inside the signpost band | Commitments cut; 8-Hi in place of 12-Hi; inventory up while growth slows | Commitments raised beyond FY29; Q4 guide > ~$124bn |
| ~20 Nov | Anthropic IPO (lab financing) | Priced at or above the marks | Pulled, or priced below the Series H | Well above the marks |
| 10 Dec (new 5 Nov) | ERCOT/PUCT data-center audit to the Texas PUC (pause on new loads ≥ 75 MW since 3 Aug) | Audit closes; energisation resumes under cost and water conditions | Pause extended into 2027, or a statutory moratorium after the Legislature meets (12 Jan) | Pause lifted with clear cost-allocation terms |
| 16 Dec | Micron FQ1-27: FQ2 guide, gross margin against the 86.25% floor, bits, FY27 capex, RPO and deposits | Margin ≥ 86% and sequential growth | Flat price increases in the FQ2 guide; margin guided down; customer pushouts | RPO > $200bn; more SCAs with floors; prices still up |
| ~7 Jan 2027 | Samsung Q4 preliminary | Operating profit about at consensus | A miss on memory prices | A beat |
| 11–19 Jan 2027 (new 5 Nov) | Governors sworn in: Ohio and Georgia (11 Jan), Texas (19 Jan); Texas Legislature from 12 Jan | Conditions and cost rules, no blanket bans | A statewide moratorium by order or statute; a hyperscaler moves or cancels ≥ 1 GW over policy | Pledges softened into cost-allocation rules |
| Late Jan (SK hynix ~28 Jan, Samsung ~29 Jan) | 2027 capex figures and Q1-27 bit and price guides | 2027 capex up moderately (~+20% for the three) | 2027 capex +30% or more with fab pull-ins: 2028–29 deeper | 2027 flat; fabs staged back |
| Late Jan–early Feb | Hyperscaler 2027 capex figures (Microsoft and Meta late Jan; Alphabet and Amazon early Feb) | K1 2027 ~1.1tn (Base) | A platform guides 2027 flat or down (K1 Bear) | > 1.3tn (Bull) |
| Mid-2027 | 2028 HBM volume and price negotiations | 2028 volumes up, prices flat to down | Prices down sharply or volumes cut: wafers return to DRAM | 2028 sold out at higher prices |
| Ongoing | Foreign flows in Kospi | — | Net selling > USD 20bn in a quarter with DRAM prices falling | Sustained net buying while contract prices still rise |
| Ongoing | CXMT | — | 4F² specs, a named customer and volume; 420k wafers a month reached early | Delays; export controls |
| Quarterly (mid-Jan for Q4) | Smartphone and PC shipments (IDC, Counterpoint) | 2027 flat to slightly down | Another double-digit fall | Recovery |

### 7. For the analyses that lead with this section

- `docs/skhynix-analysis.md`, `docs/samsung-analysis.md` and `docs/kospi-analysis.md` open with the
  five lines above and the implied path: the basket figure and their own. The IRR follows as "what
  the price implies".
- E / R against HY is shown only as a reference, not as the gate (user rule, 1 Nov).
- Each states its own multiplier: "at today's price the analysis earns k at x× the Base 2028–30
  path". That number replaces a precise IRR in the verdict, and its distance from 1.0 is the margin
  in cycle terms.
- Rows unchanged for them: the H2 EPS path, revenue index, exit P/E (8 / 10 / 11), cash conversion,
  scenarios and catalysts.

  `[?]`.

  ~4.6** (operating loss ~8.1), **Q1 2026 4.73, Q2 11.5** (another summary: 10.9), and an adjusted

  (4.6 + OpenAI ~13 `[?]`). The 2026 build holds: H1 16.2 + H2 ~37–40 on the 65bn July run-rate
  → ~55. The public S-1 (listing reported for November) will be the first audited figure.
- **22 Oct correction (OpenAI)** `[E]`: OpenAI told investors its annualised revenue was **nearly
  USD 50bn at end-September** (FT and Reuters, 8 Oct; CNBC: run-rate +77% in Q3, enterprise
  +107%). The "~70bn" used here since 12 Oct is reported as its run-rate *target* for end-2026
  (Bloomberg). OpenAI 2026 booked revenue: Q1 5.7 + Q2 6.7 + Q3 ~11 + Q4 ~15 ≈ **39** (was ~45)
  `[A]`, so **E1 2026 ≈ 94** (was ~100; −6%, below the 10% rule: the row stays at ~100 with the
  range 90–110 and is re-set at the next full update) and the exit run-rate memo ~150 (was ~170).
  Definitions differ: Anthropic's run-rate includes sales through AWS and Google Cloud, OpenAI's
  does not, so the two run-rates are not like for like. The monetisation ratio for 2026 is
  ~0.12 on the corrected figure (0.13 in the table). E1 2027–29 are unchanged: the Base already
  has growth halving from ~+90%, and OpenAI's own Q3 growth (+77% in run-rate) is inside it.
- **29 Oct (Anthropic's public S-1 of 26 Oct; Microsoft's 10-Q):**
  - Anthropic's **H1-26 revenue is USD 16.3bn** `[F]` (draft 16.2), so the 2026 build stands.

    price range and size are blank.
  - **OpenAI paid Microsoft 10.0bn in the September quarter, incl. revenue sharing** `[F]`. Its
    own booked Q3 revenue is ~11bn `[A]`, so OpenAI's spending with one cloud is about its whole
    revenue; the gap is funded by its raises (K3).
  - That 10bn sits inside E2 (Azure) and must not be added to E1.
  - E1 2026–29 unchanged.
- **E1 Base:** growth halves each year from ~+90% (2027) as the base grows and price per token
  keeps falling (Epoch AI: −47% a quarter at constant capability, secondary `[E]`). The exit
  run-rate already covers most of 2027's Base.
- **E2:** Q2 growth Azure 43% (Q1 FY27 guide 45%), Google Cloud 82%, AWS ~37%, OCI 121% `[E]`.
- **E2 2026 corrected (15 Oct, Alphabet check):** weighted by Q2 revenue (AWS ~42.3, Azure ~29.4
  `[A]` (Microsoft analysis), Google Cloud 24.8 `[F]`, OCI ~7.3 `[A]`; 103.8 a quarter, ×4 ≈ 415),
  the four clouds grew **~52% y/y in Q2** — not ~40%. Google Cloud's Q3 consensus implies ~+90%, so
  2026 as a whole is ~+50%. The 2027–29 rows are kept: Base +38% in 2027 is now a 12 pp slowdown,
  backed by backlogs (GCP RPO 513.9, >50% within 24 months `[F]`), and the company analyses add
  named premiums where the backlog supports one. **Company-level 2027 Base used downstream (memo,
  not a row):** Azure +38% (at the row, `docs/msft-analysis.md`), Google Cloud **+50%** (row +12 pp
  backlog premium, `docs/googl-analysis.md`); **AWS +38%** (at the row: backlog 2.9× run-rate against
  Google Cloud's 5.2×, capacity doubling by end-2027; `docs/amzn-analysis.md`); OCI not analysed.

  - **2026 (re-set with AWS's actuals, 1 Nov):** ~+53% (2025 base ~286bn: Azure ~86, Google
    Cloud ~59, AWS ~129, OCI ~13 `[A]`) against the row's ~+50%. This is the current year (no
    spread), and the gap of ~3 pp is below the 5 pp rule: **kept**. AWS (+36.6%) grows below the
    blend in 2026, so the hint from Amazon's report is for 2027–28 only.
  - **2028 (new, 1 Nov):** AWS on management's "over 40%" against the row's +25% would lift the
    four-cloud 2028 to ~+31% (AWS ~40% of the four, the others at the row) `[A]`, about the Bull
    (+32%). This is a company projection `[E]`, and the Amazon analysis keeps AWS at the row and
    shows the projection only as a sensitivity (12.3%). So no independent bottom-up evidence moves
    the row. It is tracked as a Bull-side signpost: AWS > 40% through 2027.
  - **2027 Base +38% kept.**
    - The bottom-up company view blends to ~+41%: Google Cloud's premium on its ~25% share; Azure
      and AWS at the row. The gap is ~3 pp.
    - Microsoft kept Azure at the row although Azure accelerates (48% → 49–50% guided), because
      its backlog did not grow. That is evidence on both sides.
    - **Run-rate test:** the four clouds' Q4-26 exit run-rate (~520bn) is ~18% above the CY26
      average. +38% in 2027 therefore needs ~6% sequential growth a quarter, after 11–16% a quarter
      in 2026 (Q2 +16%, Q3 +13%, Q4 ~+12% `[A]`). The Q4-27 exit growth y/y would be ~+26%. That is
      a sharp slowdown but within the digestion the Base describes. A Base at the Q4-26 exit pace
      would be far above the Bull (+45%).
    - For Google Cloud alone the plain row would mean ~3% a quarter, which is why its analysis
      carries a premium.
  - **Open checkpoint (structural):** a per-cloud split of E2, which Alphabet's analysis
    suggests, would change a row definition. Under this skill that is a method change, not an
    update step, so it is left for the next full re-baseline. Until then this memo table carries
    the per-cloud view.

## Interface — monetisation ratio (E1 ÷ K1)

| Bear / **Base** / Bull | 2025 | 2026 | 2027 | 2028 | 2029 |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Lab revenue ÷ hyperscaler capex | 0.04 | **0.13** | 0.16 / **0.17** / 0.18 | 0.21 / **0.24** / 0.24 | 0.23 / **0.31** / 0.30 |

- It rises in every scenario: in Bear because capex is cut, in Base and Bull because revenue
  grows faster than capex. **The ratio says the build-out is not yet paid for in any scenario
  within the window;** the hyperscalers' own AI revenue (Copilot, Gemini, ads) and internal use
  (Meta) are not in it, so it understates the true payback.
- Base 2028–29 (275 / 1,170 = 0.235; 360 / 1,170 = 0.308) now sits at or above the Bull
  (370 / 1,550 = 0.239; 515 / 1,700 = 0.303), because the Base plateau holds capex flat while lab
  revenue grows. That shows the limit of a flow ratio: it rises in every scenario and misses the
  depreciation of the capital stock, so it cannot tell a sustainable path from an unsustainable
  one. Read it for direction; the durability measure is the **K1 return gauge** (K1 capex cycle
  section, coverage 70% / 63% of the revenue needed for 10% pre-tax in 2028–29).

  and the exit on 2030 EPS. 2027 EPS enters only through the 2027 dividend (payout 34%) and the
  retained cash ((c − payout) × EPS = −0.54 × EPS). A lower 2027 EPS therefore also means less net
  debt built. Over 2027 the two offset (+0.01 pp).
