## Scenario definitions

| Scenario | Weight (convention) | Equity BBB scenario | AI catalyst(s) | Mechanism | Earliest | Signposts |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| **Bear** | 25% | Bear (recession H1 2027 + oil Bear) | **Financing break** + monetisation disappointment; memory overshoot 2028 | Lab revenue growth stalls (price war, enterprise budgets cut); a lab misses commitments; Oracle/neocloud contracts renegotiated; AI credit tightens; hyperscalers cut 2028 capex 16%; GPU and memory prices fall | H1 2027 | Lab run-rate growth < 10% q/q for two quarters · a hyperscaler guides next-year capex flat/down · Oracle cut to BB+ or RPO falls · capex/OCF > 100% with debt share > 40% · DRAM contract prices fall q/q · new vendor loans or guarantees > USD 50bn in a quarter, or one called |
| **Base** | 50% | Base (AI boom slows to trend) | Contracted 2027 build, then digestion | 2027 capex +42% on backlog and supply limits; 2028–29 a digestion plateau (+4.5%, 0%) while the 2026–27 build earns in; lab revenue ~3.6× by 2029; memory normalises | Ongoing | Capex guidance within ±10% · NVIDIA within its guide · lab run-rate +15–30% q/q · BTOS rising ~1 pt a quarter |
| **Bull** | 25% | Bull (soft landing) | **Agentic adoption** | Adoption broadens; tokens per user multiply; lab revenue +130% in 2027; capex to ~1.3–1.7tn; supply limits turn demand into prices and margins | Q2 2027 | 2027 capex consensus > USD 1.3tn · lab run-rate > USD 250bn by mid-2027 · Copilot seats > 70m · BTOS > 30% |

- The AI Bear is inside the equity Bear: a recession alone slows but does not break contracted 2027
  builds; the financing break is what turns 2028 negative.
- Tail risks not modelled: Taiwan conflict; export-control escalation (NVIDIA already assumes no
  China data-center compute revenue `[F]`).

## Drivers

### US midterms: the policy channels (note 5 Nov 2026)

**Question:** does the 3 Nov result change K1, its financing or the layer revenues for 2027–29?
Each channel is tested against what is stated, what can pass, and when.

| Channel | What is stated or proposed | What can happen, and when | Rows and years | Moves Base? |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Federal: a data-center moratorium | Sanders and Ocasio-Cortez's AI Data Center Moratorium Act (Mar 2026): no new construction until federal AI law protects workers, consumers and the environment. Sanders renewed the call after the vote `[E]` | It cannot become law over a veto and needs 60 Senate votes. Democrats lack a veto-proof majority (Oil BBB, 4 Nov) | K1 | No |
| Federal: grid-cost allocation | The Ratepayer Protection Act (Husted) passed the House 417–3, but Senate cloture failed 57–43 on 30 Sep. Democrats called it optional and want binding cost recovery. The hyperscalers pledged at the White House (Mar 2026) to pay for new generation `[E]` | A binding version can draw votes from both parties from 3 Jan 2027 and fits the President's own pledge `[A]` | Cost per GW (K1 dollars, H5), not volume | No |
| Federal: tax | Permanent expensing (2025 law); the CHIPS 48D credit (35%) for construction begun by end-2026 `[E]` | A repeal or an extension needs the President: none is expected `[A]` | K3 (cash taxes) | No |
| Federal: export controls | Senior Democrats (Warren, Meeks) object to NVIDIA's H200 licence for China; the GAIN AI and SAFE Chips bills `[E]` | They could ride the FY28 NDAA, a must-pass bill, in 2027 `[A]`. NVIDIA's guidance already excludes China data-center compute `[F]` | H1 (China tail row) | No |
| Federal: oversight, appropriations, confirmations | Hearings and subpoenas ("Lawyer up": Garcia, the presumptive Oversight chair); Pallone in line for Energy and Commerce. The FY27 stopgap ends on 11 Dec and is decided by the current Congress. FY28 riders are possible from Oct 2027, and the Senate confirms FERC and NRC nominees from January `[E]` | Headlines and delays, not paths | K1 (sentiment) | No |
| **States: Texas** | ~27% of planned US data-center GW (Wells Fargo) `[E]`. Abbott's pause since 3 Aug: no energisation of new loads ≥ 75 MW without their own generation until an ERCOT/PUCT audit of the ~192 GW Batch Zero cohort; TCEQ permits halted since 21 Sep (enforced 19 Oct); audit due 10 Dec `[E]`. Hinojosa (sworn in 19 Jan) pledges a moratorium until the Legislature (from 12 Jan) passes protections, and an end to the tax breaks `[E]` | Her levers are agency directives and the PUC appointments. A statutory ban or a tax-break repeal needs the Legislature. The pause predates the Q3 capex guides | K1 2028–29 (new US sites), H4, H5 | No: a risk to 2028 |
| **States: Georgia, Ohio, others** | Georgia (~7%): Lance Bottoms pledged a temporary statewide pause. Ohio (~5%): Acton backs a conditional moratorium (100% of utility costs, union labour). Pennsylvania ~5%. Voters rejected data centers in 40 of 41 local ballot measures (Ohio 21) `[E]` | From 11 Jan. Utility regulators are appointed over time (BofA: "more durable than a campaign-only issue"). Siting gets slower and costlier | K1 2028–29 | No: a risk to 2028 |
| Companies | No guidance change found from a hyperscaler, NVIDIA or a utility. They answer with concessions: the Amazon Data Center Commitment, Microsoft's Community-First plan, Google's USD 1bn in local programmes by 2027, NDAs dropped `[E]` | The next read-outs are NVIDIA (17 Nov) and the 2027 capex figures (late Jan–early Feb) | K1 | No |
| Sell side | No published hyperscaler capex cut was found. Ives: a "more measured pace", still > USD 1tn in 2027. Wells Fargo: "a left-tail event to the AI trade". UBS: ~30% of 2028 capacity at risk, from a secondary source; Goldman's ~45% is unverified `[E]`/`[?]` | — | K1 2028 | No |

**Reading:**
- **The market reads lower capex growth: it bought the payers and sold the receivers.** From 30 Oct
  to 5 Nov Amazon is +8%, Microsoft +3% and Alphabet +2%, while Micron is −24%, Vertiv −24%, AMD
  −18%, TSMC −12%, NVIDIA −11% and CoreWeave −21%. Needham calls the platforms "AI incumbents" that
  gain from higher barriers `[E]`. No company has said that capex will be lower.
- **The 2027 build holds.** It is contracted, mostly under construction, and power-limited; capacity
  energising in 2027 already has its connections `[A]`. The Texas pause was in force when Microsoft,
  Alphabet, Amazon and Meta guided 2027 up.
- **2028 carries the risk,** and the Base now holds a digestion plateau there (+4.5%; K1 capex
  cycle section). New sites in the pause states slip or move to other states and abroad, and cost
  more. The illustrative sizing in the 5 Nov note (~3–4% of K1 2028 `[A]`, an upper bound now that
  the Base 2028 builds fewer new sites) is below the 5% minimum change and inside the Base range
  (1,000–1,350).
  A statutory moratorium in Texas, or a hyperscaler moving or cancelling ≥ 1 GW over policy, would
  be the evidence to move it.
- **Where the risk lands:** on the hardware layers through units (H1, H2, H4) and on H5. A cap on new
  US sites raises the value of capacity that is already built, which supports E2 pricing. That is
  the Needham argument, and the market's reading on 4 Nov.
- **Scenario fit:** state siting policy is a second route to the Bear's 2028 capex cut, beside the
  financing break. It is added as a signpost. The scenario definitions are not changed (that would
  be a method change), and 25/50/25 stays a convention.

- **Reading:** the sell-off is concentrated where the Base is most cyclical (memory, equipment,
  AI credit) and in Korea and Japan, where AI hardware is a large share of the index. Platforms
  and software did not fall. That is a **re-pricing of the hardware cycle and its financing**, not
  a loss of faith in AI demand. UBS: "a recalibration of expectations rather than a fundamental
  break" `[E]`. Prior episodes this year (16–17 and 29 Jul: CXMT parent listing, Kioxia −13%,
  Nikkei −5%) reversed within weeks `[E]`.
- **What would make it fundamental:** next-year capex guidance flat or down at the Q3 reports
  (27–30 Oct), DRAM contract prices falling, or a failed or delayed Anthropic/OpenAI financing.
  None has happened.

### Money in
- Capex guidance has risen every quarter; guided 2026 spending is over 150% above initial
  estimates `[E]`. The 2027 consensus (~1.1tn) is the brokers' floor; Goldman calls it too
  conservative (1.2tn, upside 1.4tn) `[E]`.
- **Funding has changed:** debt now funds about a third of the increase; Alphabet and Oracle sold
  equity; lease commitments (~820bn) sit off balance sheet; Oracle is one notch above high yield.
  Vendor and circular financing (chip makers and clouds investing in labs that buy their compute)
  is large: **≥ USD 270bn of loans, guarantees and equity commitments** (K3; 215 on 17 Oct, 230
  on 22 Oct with Amazon's facility, 220 on 29 Oct after Google's commitment was corrected to 30bn,
  270 on 1 Nov with Amazon's 50bn OpenAI Series C),
  led by NVIDIA's up-to-105bn guaranty for OpenAI's Ohio campus. **Q3 added the first platform
  equity sale beyond withholding (Alphabet's 12.4bn ATM)**, and Microsoft says it will tap
  "additional sources of funding". No public series nets it out of demand
  `[?]`; the K3 rows are this baseline's own count.

### Hardware
- Supply, not demand, limits 2026–27: CoWoS, HBM and power are sold out or short. That supports
  2027 volumes and prices in every scenario and shifts the risk to 2028.
- NVIDIA: Q2 FY27 revenue 96.2bn (+106% y/y), data center 89.0bn (+117%), gross margin 75.0%,
  Q3 guide 108.0bn ±2%; Vera Rubin in full production; inventories 31.6bn (from 21.4bn in Jan)
  `[F]`. Custom silicon gains (Google TPU systems sold externally from Q2 2026; Broadcom AI
  +221% y/y; AMD MI450/Helios with OpenAI, Meta, Anthropic) `[E]`.

### Engines
- Two labs dominate visible revenue, and both grow faster than any software company has: Anthropic
  from ~4.6bn booked revenue in 2025 (draft S-1) to a 65bn run-rate (July 2026); OpenAI to **~50bn at end-September** (FT/Reuters; the ~70bn first reported is its end-2026 target) `[E]`.
- Their commitments are larger still: OpenAI targets ~600bn of compute spending by 2030 and
  projects ~115bn of cash burn through 2029 `[E]`. **The labs' ability to keep raising money is
  the hinge between engines and hardware.**

### Tools
- Copilots ramp (Microsoft 365 Copilot 15m → 30m paid seats Jan → Jul 2026 `[E]`) and pricing
  moves to consumption. AI-native tools grow fastest (coding agents). Seat-based software faces
  substitution; its multiples have already compressed (Adobe 8.8×, Intuit 11×, Salesforce 14×
  forward P/E `[F]`), while Palantir (89×) and Snowflake (123×) price the winners.
- Gartner places GenAI in the "trough of disillusionment" in 2026 and expects AI mostly bought as
  features of existing software `[E]` (Gartner total AI spending 2.7tn in 2026, +49.5%; AI
  infrastructure 1.48tn → 1.98tn in 2027 `[E]`, very broad definitions; cross-check only).

  comes from the Equity BBB of 6 Nov (stale after 6 Dec) and HY from the HY BBB of 5 Nov (stale
  after 5 Dec; its 11 Nov note kept NIBOR and the reference).
- **On the HY rule (E / R against the HY market reference 0.254):**
  - **For memory the rule is not the gate** (user, 1 Nov; Memory cycle section). Memory is 0.867 on
    the decision Bear and 0.195 capped; the two Korean analyses were 0.215 and 0.249 capped at their
    run prices (not re-run at today's).
  - No other basket reaches 0.254: foundry 0.123, platforms 0.110, compute 0.068. Foundry and
    platforms are above World (0.080); compute is now below it.
  - Foundry (10.2%) and platforms (8.6%) give more than World (6.6%) and the HY market (7.2%);
    compute (7.5%) is about the HY market. Their Bear 2027 loss is 1.8× (platforms) to 2.3×
    (compute) World's. Compute's fall is the K1 re-set (method), not price.
  - The AI case is still a bet on growth after 2027 that the Base does not include: compute needs
    ~3% a year of EPS growth after 2027 for 12%, while its Base now digests at about −2% a year to
    2030 (.92 → .87); platforms need ~15% a year.
- **Triggers (where a layer starts to pay at k):**
  - Baskets, at a CY27 P/E of: compute ~12.1 (−11.8% from 13.7 now); platforms ~19.1 (−9.1%);
    foundry and equipment ~16.5 (−4.7%); memory ~3.38 (2.64 now: +28%, cleared). In cycle terms:
    compute needs 1.14× its Base 2028–30 (K1 capex cycle, section 5); memory clears at 0.75× Base
    2028–30.
  - Companies (P_k from each analysis, at its run price):
    - Clear: NVIDIA $212.1 at $168.89 (+25.6%; ≈ $192, +13.4%, on the re-set H1 path `[A]`),
      Micron ~$752 at $656.39 (v3; +14.5%), SK hynix KRW ~1,836,000 and Samsung ~306,000 (≈ +62% at
      the 6 Nov close).
    - Fail: TSMC $369.8 at $396.48 (v1.2; −6.7%), Microsoft $495.9 at $530.61 (−6.5%), Alphabet
      $262.3 at $284.78 (−7.9%), Amazon $222.2 at $270.87 (−17.9%). The K1 re-set lifts the three
      platforms' P_k by ≈ $3–11 `[A]`; at the upper bound Microsoft (−4.4%) and Alphabet (−4.7%)
      would enter the ±5% band.

- **K1 capex cycle signposts (new 11 Nov):** four rows above (K1 2027 first figures, the return
  gauge, GPU rental indices, FCF after shareholder returns), dated in the K1 capex cycle section,

  below −50bn), rental prices still rise, and the gauge sits at 70% for 2028.
- **Signposts after the 11 Nov note: none crossed.**
  - **Neocloud funding:** CoreWeave's Q3 leans Base. Revenue, backlog, guide and prepayments are on
    the Base side. The stock is back above its low ($48.73), and the unsecured market is shut at
    current yields. Neither line is met.
  - **TSMC monthly:** October +59.7% y/y, a second month above +45%. The line is set into 2027, so
    it is not crossed.
  - **Lab financing:** Anthropic's range ($70–78) is ×1.19–1.32 the Series H per share. A pricing
    inside it (~18 Nov) crosses the Bull line, which triggers a re-test. Lab run-rates: booked Q3
    +47% q/q.
  - **Vendor financing:** the Class N placement adds at most ~16.5bn of new supplier equity, below
    the 50bn flow line.
  - **Export rules beyond China:** none published; dates 18 Nov (House briefing) and 18–19 Nov
    (Trump–Xi).
- **Signposts after Update 7 (7 Nov): none crossed.**
  - **Neocloud funding:** CoreWeave's closing low was $43.66 on 5 Nov ($46.40 on 6 Nov). The
    equity leg is crossed; the credit leg is not measured, so the signpost stays touched. Its Q3
    (10 Nov) is the next read-out.
  - **Memory:** NAND wafer spot fell 4.3% in a week and DDR4 spot stalled above contract
    (TrendForce, 4 Nov). This is spot, not the contract signpost, and it leans to the early side.
  - **Reports re-tested:** AMD (3 Nov) and Arista (3 Nov) are on the Base-to-Bull side for 2026–27
    (AMD's Q4 guide +27% q/q; Arista's 2027 +38–40%), but neither meets a minimum change.
  - Next: CoreWeave Q3 (10 Nov), TSMC October revenue (~10 Nov), the Samsung and SK hynix Q3
    business reports (~14 and ~16 Nov), NVIDIA Q3 FY27 (17 Nov) and the Anthropic IPO (roadshow
    reported from the week of 9 Nov, pricing ~20 Nov).
- **Signposts after the first 5 Nov note: none crossed; one first leg touched.**
  - **Neocloud funding:** CoreWeave made a new 2026 low on 5 Nov. The credit-spread leg was not
    measured, so the signpost was read as touched, not crossed.
  - **New:** US data-center siting policy and federal AI law (both Bear-side risks to K1 2028), with
    dates 10 Dec, 3 Jan and 11–19 Jan.
  - **Next read-out:** NVIDIA Q3 FY27 on 17 Nov. This is the first company statement after the vote;
    watch the Q4 guide, the supply commitments and any comment on US siting.
- **Signposts after Update 6 (1 Nov): none newly crossed; one first leg crossed.**
  - **Capex ÷ OCF:** the five ran at ~116% in Q3 (Amazon 179%). The debt leg is above 40% at Amazon
    and Alphabet, but it is not measured for the five, so the signpost is read as touched.
  - **K4:** AWS +16.5% took the total to ~2,480. Only Microsoft is flat, so the Bear line (two of
    four flat or down) is not reached.
  - **Bull side:** AWS accelerates (+39.3%), and its CEO projects "over 40%" for 2027 and 2028.
    For AWS alone that is near the E2 Bull for 2027 (+45%) and above it for 2028 (+32%). It is
    guidance, so it is a signpost, not a move.
  - **Memory:** no Bear signpost crossed (no q/q contract decline, no range renegotiation). The
    momentum signals sit on the early side of Base: q/q growth halved twice, mobile DRAM 0–5%,
    NAND MLC flat in October. The dated memory signposts are in the Memory cycle section
    (table 6).
  - The vendor-financing level rose to ≥ 270bn by a correction (Amazon's OpenAI Series C), not by a
    new flow.
- **Signposts after Update 5 (29 Oct): one crossed, on the Bear side.**
  - **Platform equity funding:** Alphabet's Q3 ATM sales (12.4bn, beyond withholding) are the first
    platform equity issue for funding since this signpost was set. With Meta's Class C, Oracle's
    20bn sale and Microsoft's "additional sources of funding", the financing break's first stage
    (platforms needing external capital) is visible.
  - This is a recalibration trigger, and this update is the re-test. It does not by itself move a
    Base point: Alphabet's dilution is already in its bottom-up EPS, which moved the E 2027 point.
    The 25/50/25 weights are a convention and are not changed.
  - **Touched, not crossed:**
    - Microsoft's RPO was flat q/q (K4: two of four flat or down is the Bear line).
    - Capex ÷ OCF is ~99% for four of the five; the debt share is not updated.
  - **Bull side:** Azure accelerating (48% → 49–50% guided) and Google Cloud +103% are on the Bull
    side of E2, but only for one quarter.
  - Lab financing: the Anthropic IPO (week of 16 Nov) is the next test.
- **Signposts after Update 4 (24 Oct):** none crossed. Closer on the Bear side: NVIDIA's CEO now
  names neocloud capital as the constraint (the start of this baseline's financing break), and
  CoreWeave has halved from its high. The Bull side of the lab-financing signpost is two to four
  weeks away (Anthropic's reported listing at up to twice its Series H mark).
- **Signposts after Update 3 (22 Oct):** none crossed. Meta's report is on the Base side (revenue
  +26%, capex rising, no 2027 cut). The financing signposts moved closer: Meta joins Alphabet in
  preparing equity funding (non-voting Class C "for capital raising"), Meta's Q3 capex exceeded its
  operating cash flow, and vendor financing is ≥ USD 230bn with Amazon's Anthropic facility.
  OpenAI's run-rate correction (50bn, not 70bn) does not cross the lab signpost (+77% in Q3).
- (17 Oct) The old vendor-financing threshold ("loans to labs > USD 100bn combined") was exceeded
  once NVIDIA's August guaranty counts; it predates the 12 Oct baseline, so it was reset to a flow
  signpost rather than read as a new crossing.
- Closest to Bear (1 Nov):
  - capital as the stated constraint (Broadcom, NVIDIA), and the platforms' capex above operating
    cash flow (the five at ~116% in Q3);
  - vendor financing ≥ USD 270bn;
  - one platform selling equity (Alphabet), with two more prepared (Meta's Class C, Oracle's 20bn);
  - Microsoft's backlog flat;
  - the AI-credit names: CoreWeave −49% from its high;
  - in memory, the deceleration of contract prices (Memory cycle section).

  Bull side: the cloud growth rates (Azure, Google Cloud, and now AWS with its 2027–28
  projection).

**Before the next update (open checkpoints):**
1. **Memory (Memory cycle section, table 6):**
   - LTA ranges, floors and deposits in the Q3 business reports (~14 Nov Samsung, ~16 Nov SK
     hynix);
   - the November and December contract prints and TrendForce's Q1-27 outlook;
   - NVIDIA's supply commitments (17 Nov) and Micron's FQ1-27 (16 Dec);
   - the 2027 memory capex figures (late January).

   A disclosed floor, a q/q price decline or a capex figure is the kind of independent evidence
   that can move an H2 point.
2. **E-layer points to watch:** Base 2028 (blend gap −0.037) and Bear 2027 (−0.046; −0.063 with
   Meta on the analysed Bear). A Meta analysis would settle the Bear.
3. **E2 per-cloud split (structural):** this is a row-definition change, so it is a method change
   left for the next full re-baseline. The memo table under Engines carries the per-cloud view
   until then, now with AWS's actuals. AWS's "over 40%" for 2028 is the first company projection
   above the E2 Bull.
4. **Mid-cycle anchor (method, new 1 Nov):** under the layer method a rise in peak-year (CY27)
   consensus lifts 2028–30 one for one. SK hynix's CY27 is +12.9% since 16 Oct, and the basket's
   +6.4%. Whether the 2030 mid-cycle should be anchored to a fixed basis rather than to the latest
   peak-year consensus is a method question for the re-baseline. The effect now is ~2 pp of the
   memory Base, or ~5% of price.
5. **Alphabet Q3 10-Q** (early November): Cloud backlog `[F]` (561bn via the call `[E]`), the
   Anthropic share, the equity-securities split.
6. **Basket method:** retained cash tied to EPS makes the 2027 EPS point nearly irrelevant for
   layers with c < 0 (E). Whether to tie it to capex is for the re-baseline.

    Texas, Georgia and Ohio, change K1, K3 or the layer revenues for 2027–29?

    over a veto after it. The state channel is real but slow (governors from 11–19 Jan; statutes need
    the legislatures), and the Texas pause (since 3 Aug) was already in force when the hyperscalers
    guided 2027 up. No company changed guidance and no broker capex cut was found. The risk to K1

    75,000 (29 Dec 2022); Micron $29.02 (24 Dec 2018), $48.88 (26 Sep 2022).
  - Micron quarterly (Alpha Vantage): operating income peaked at $4.38bn in FQ4-18 (to 31 Aug 2018;
    gross margin 61.0%) and $3.00bn in FQ3-22 (to 31 May 2022). It troughed at $0.52bn in FQ1-20
    and −$2.30bn in FQ2-23.
